# Tasks

Context before anything else, because rows below borrow vocabulary from several
real disciplines: **this whole file is WORLDBUILDING.** Vesper is a fictional
planet, and a task here is engineering work on the simulation of it -- a toy
climate model, a terrain generator, a soil model, and their bookkeeping. Words
like flux, dust, ice, rainfall and albedo name modelled quantities of an
invented world; nothing in this file refers to the real one. The nearest
real-world analogue of this file is the issue tracker for a game world's
physics engine.

Atomic, trackable work items. **Findings documents do not carry their own to-do
lists** -- an audit says what is true, this file says what to do about it, and
the two are kept apart so a finding can be read without being re-litigated and a
task can be closed without editing the argument that produced it.

Every task cites the document that justifies it. If a task has no source, it is
not yet a finding and probably needs one.

Status: `open` | `doing` | `blocked` | `done` | `wontfix` (with a reason).

## Conventions

- **One table per prefix, and every id it has ever issued appears in it.** That
  is what the tables are FOR: the next id in an area is the bottom of its table
  plus one, read in one place, with no max to compute across two files. Ids are
  stable and never reused, and a reused id makes every citation of it ambiguous
  forever. This structure exists because taking the max over six prefixes and
  not the seventh issued a duplicate HYD-17.
- **A closed row STAYS, sanitised to its outcome and a pointer.** The reasoning
  that closed it lives in `archive/tasks.md`; what stays here is that the id is
  spent and how it went. So this file answers "what is left" and "what may I
  number next" without being a second copy of the archive.
- Prefixes name an area and are listed as section headings below.
- One line per task. If it needs a paragraph, it needs a findings document.
- A task that turns out to be wrong is closed `wontfix` with the reason, not
  deleted. The reasoning is the point, and it goes to the archive.
- **A task names the step it touches**, as `[step: <id>]` in its status column,
  where the id is from `config/pipeline.yaml`. It says WHERE the work lands and
  is annotation, not a verdict. The carve gate -- does anything outstanding
  still move what the verdict is computed from -- is a judgement made by reading
  this file, because the answer is what closing a row would CHANGE and that is
  in the row's own prose. Computing it from these markers was tried and removed:
  a marker names a location, not an effect, so the two are not interchangeable
  at any count. `pipeline.py` reads no tracker, so nothing written here can
  change what the pipeline planner reports. A task that names none is not ignored -- it is
  reported as a RESIDUAL to be arbitrated, because the graph narrows the
  judgement and does not replace it. Name more than one where it applies.
  A row whose status begins `done` or `wontfix` is spent, wherever it sits;
  that is what a reader skips.

- **Regenerating a derived artifact is a STEP, not a task.** It belongs in the
  ordering in `docs/src/pipeline/sequencing.md`, beside the run that consumes it. A row
  that says "rebuild X before the next run" is tracking state, and state is what
  `check_consistency.py` and `world_state.json` are for. HYD-16 was such a row
  and should never have been one. CLAUDE.md rule 7.
- **A task is code, physics, a decision or a measurement that settles a
  mechanism.** If closing it would produce only a number that the next iteration
  regenerates, it is not a task; the durable half is whatever it teaches.

---

THE PORTABLE OBJECT IS THREE EQUATIONS, AND IT NEEDS CHANNEL STORAGE FIRST, 2026-08-22. Neither standard model transfers whole and they fail differently. IMAGE-GNM has the right SHAPE for a routed world -- in-water retention as nutrient spiralling, `R = 1 - exp(-vf/HL)` with `HL = D/tau` and `tau = V/Q`, which is TWO NUMBERS plus a temperature modifier -- but produces only total N and total P, with speciation named as future work. Global NEWS 2 SPECIATES, giving exactly this row's dissolved and particulate split, and has NO ROUTING at all: lumped basin-to-mouth export fractions calibrated against 40 to 108 Earth basins, whose numeric coefficients are in Supplementary Tables NOT present in the PDF held here. So take IMAGE-GNM's three equations -- but they need volume and depth per cell, which means they need WET-2's channel storage first. RETENTION HAS NO REPRESENTATION TO BE WRONG UNTIL TRANSIENT STORAGE EXISTS. Note also that CaMa's own tracer scheme is a pure passive advector with no reaction term: transport free, retention not at all. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 52c |

AND THE LEAF-TO-CANOPY BOUND UNDER IT IS NOT ONE-SIGNED, 2026-08-22. `analysis/vegetation_albedo.py:33-35` asserts without argument that "a canopy treatment would give a smaller ratio than a leaf one", and the bracket runs from no correction to the leaf ratio on that basis. CANOPY ALBEDO DEPENDS ON THE SINGLE-SCATTERING ALBEDO `alpha + tau`, NOT ON REFLECTANCE ALONE: a leaf transmitting strongly in the near-infrared raises omega_NIR without raising omega_PAR, so the canopy keeps more of its NIR albedo relative to PAR than the leaf does, and since a K dwarf moves flux INTO the near-infrared that AMPLIFIES the correction rather than diluting it. ClimaLand's `pfts.jl` carries the missing column, CLM5.0 Table 2.3.1 giving `tau_NIR/alpha_NIR` of 0.286 needleleaf, 0.556 broadleaf tree and 0.971 GRASS. Running this project's own band pair through the semi-infinite similarity result gives canopy k25v/sun ratios of 1.1379, 1.1848 and 1.2226 against the leaf-level 1.1501 -- SO THE STATED BOUND HOLDS FOR ONE OF THREE CLASSES AND FAILS FOR THE OTHER TWO, grass included, which is where this project's per-class table already shows the largest shift at 0.303 to 0.333. THIS DOES NOT GIVE A CANOPY RATIO and must not be used as one: the similarity result carries no leaf angle distribution, no clumping, no soil background, no finite LAI and no direct/diffuse split, and the transmittances are an Earth calibration. It establishes only that the SIGN is not determined by leaf reflectance alone, so the bracket's upper end may not be an upper end. Settling it needs leaf TRANSMITTANCE, which a reflectance library does not carry -- a data gap rather than a derivation gap. THE CLASS DEPENDENCE RUNS ALONG THIS ROW'S OWN TREE/GRASS SPLIT, which makes the separate endmembers more necessary than the row currently argues. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 38 |

| BIO-25 | Generate a stellar-spectrum- and photosystem-window-weighted photon conversion constant beside `VESPER_FRADPAR`, use it in LPJ photosynthesis, and require the combined `FRADPAR * CQ` photon supply to reproduce the registered productivity calculation | `biosphere/notes/implicit-earth-assumptions.md` finding 5 | open; LPJ currently converts the K-star's PAR energy with a fixed 550 nm Earth coefficient, so the executable and prediction use different light currencies [step: lpj_run]. MAGNITUDE MEASURED 2026-08-22, which this row did not carry. Integrating `k25v_hr.dat` against a 5772 K Planck: over 400-700 nm the solar value is 4.567e-6 mol/J, which REPRODUCES the shipped 4.6e-6 and validates the integral before anything is read off the rest; over the declared 400-750 nm window it is 4.743e-6 on the Sun and 4.864e-6 on this star. So the fixed constant is about 5.7 percent low, and the error DECOMPOSES: roughly 3.1 points is a window mismatch this project introduced when `FRADPAR` moved from 0.3963 to 0.4624 on the widening to 400-750 nm while `CQ` stayed on a 400-700 basis, and about 2.6 points is the stellar shift. One-signed: more photons per joule than assumed, so absorbed photon flux and assimilation are understated. The window half would exist even on Earth's spectrum, which is worth stating because it means the pair must be recomputed over a COMMON window rather than only re-starred. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 24 |

BIO-21 owns natural seasonal timing, BIO-27 crop timing and the 30-degree crop fallback, WET-2/WET-6 the 40 N wetland split, and FIRE-6 the 30/50-degree fire tuning; cross-check PHYS-13 outside the biosphere rather than duplicating it | `biosphere/notes/implicit-earth-assumptions.md` finding 8 | open; correctness guard and source-only work requiring no build or LPJ-GUESS run; dormant replacements retain their owning activation gates [step: lpj_driver, lpj_run]. ONE BRANCH INVENTORIED WITH ITS CONSEQUENCE PRICED, 2026-08-22. `framework/guess.cpp:1110` splits the single `PEATLAND` landcover class at `PEATLAND_WETLAND_LATITUDE_LIMIT = 40.0`: `is_highlatitude_peatland_stand()` is `lat >= 40`, `is_true_wetland_stand()` is `lat < 40`. The consequence is in `somdynam.cpp`. The high-latitude branch sets `moist_mod = RMOIST = 0.4` blending toward `RMOIST_ANAEROBIC = 0.025` past an acrotelm limit of 7.5 kgC/m2, AND sets `moist_mod_saturated`, which `:599` applies to the slow and passive pools so their effective modifier is exactly 0.025. The low-latitude branch sets `moist_mod = moisture_modifier(100) = 0.36` for every pool and leaves `moist_mod_saturated` at 1.0, so the slow and passive pools NEVER get the anaerobic treatment. ON THE LONG-LIVED POOLS THE BRANCH IS WORTH 0.36 AGAINST 0.025, A FACTOR OF 14.4, and those are the pools that decide whether peat accumulates; the fast pools are close at 0.4 against 0.36 and diverge only as carbon builds. A 40-degree line is an Earth climatology proxy and this world has 32 degrees of obliquity, a 182.8-day orbit and a K-dwarf spectrum. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 34. AND THE SAME FAILURE CLASS IS GENERIC TO THIS MODEL FAMILY, NOT A DEFECT OF THIS FORK, 2026-08-22. FATES carries the calendar TWICE -- a compile-time `days_per_year = 365.00` used 146 times, and a host-supplied runtime value used 21 times -- AND MIXES THEM INSIDE ONE PROCESS: cohort density is decremented with the runtime value while the litter from those same dead plants uses the compile-time one, so on a 145-day year the plants killed and the litter they produce differ by 365/145. It also has exactly two latitude branches, both in cold phenology, resetting counters on day 270 and day 181 -- **UNREACHABLE on a ~145-day year, so those counters are never reset and accumulate without bound**, which is a worse failure than a wrong answer. Reading a second model does not solve this fork's port; it establishes that the class is generic to Ecosystem Demography. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 54b |

CLIMBER-X has NO atmospheric chemistry, but `src/ch4/ch4_model.f90:179-183` carries a reduced form this row can take the STRUCTURE of: reciprocal lifetimes added over four sinks -- OH, tropospheric chlorine, soil and stratosphere -- with OH represented by a fitted log-linear surrogate in the methane burden plus NOx, CO and VOC emissions and a linear temperature term. IT IS A SURROGATE FOR OH, NOT OH, and its anchors are two independent Earth baselines, 1056 ppb and 286.83 K. Take the reciprocal-addition shape and the sink decomposition; re-fit or bracket every coefficient. NOTE WHAT IT DOES NOT SUPPLY: the VOC term is a prescribed anthropogenic emission series read from file, so this gives a consumption interface and no producer, and ozone there is a prescribed radiative boundary condition that is never an oxidant. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 42a. A SECOND REUSABLE FORM, BETTER CONDITIONED, AND ONE FAMILY TO REFUSE, 2026-08-22. cGENIE's `genie-atchem` has six CH4 sink schemes and NONE is photolysis -- one well-mixed box, no OH, the source saying "omitting [OH], [NOx] etc etc". TAKE THE POWER LAWS IN PARTIAL PRESSURE: `tau = tau0*(pCH4/C0)**N`, with `schmidt03` the better-conditioned variant, namelist-settable and calibrated over 1x to 200x C0 against the default's 8x. REFUSE THE POLYNOMIAL EMULATORS: `claire06` and `goldblatt06` fit photochemical output as polynomials in log10 of ABSOLUTE MOLE INVENTORIES in Tmol, not mixing ratios, which anchors them to Earth's atmospheric mass -- 1.088x here per section 42b -- and the source itself warns "neither O2 nor CH4 are truncated at edge of training values here, so USE CAUTION". A TRAP EVEN IF ADOPTING THE DEFAULT: `par_atm_CH4_photochem` defaults to a scheme that reads only compile-time constants, so the three namelist `par_pCH4_oxidation_*` values are SILENTLY IGNORED unless the scheme string is also changed, while the run log prints them as though in force. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 46a. AND A THIRD MODEL, THIS ONE WITH ACTUAL CHEMISTRY, 2026-08-22. Where cGENIE and CLIMBER-X offer parametric lifetimes to take the SHAPE of, CAABA/MECCA offers 2,323 gas-phase reactions of which 414 are photolysis, with OH, O1D and the quenching competition explicit. That makes it the instrument for deriving this row's oxidant bracket rather than another source to fit one from. Its photolysis door and the gravity correction it needs are in `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 48 |

SEA ICE TAKES AN ARITHMETIC MEAN WHERE SERIES CONDUCTION REQUIRES A HARMONIC ONE: `icemod.f90:1427` sets `zckap = (CKAPSN*h_snow + CKAPI*h_ice)/(h_snow + h_ice)` and uses it at `:1439`, where snow over ice is two conductors in series and the effective conductivity is `(h_s+h_i)/(h_s/k_s + h_i/k_i)`. With 0.31 against 2.03 W/m/K the forms diverge fast: +21 percent at 0.05 m of snow on 1 m of ice, +39 at 0.10, +83 at 0.30, +104 at 0.50. **`landmod.f90:751-752` solves the identical series problem with the correct harmonic form**, so two modules of one model disagree. AND LAND TAKES A HARMONIC MEAN WHERE AN EXTENSIVE BLEND REQUIRES AN ARITHMETIC ONE: `landmod.f90:743-744` blends snow and soil heat capacity in the top layer harmonically, and heat capacity is extensive, so the blend is `(C_sn*z_sn + C_so*z_so)/z_top`. | CLIM-49 | Settle on the record what the production build initialises reals to. The flag line carries `-ffpe-trap=invalid,zero,overflow` and `-finit-real=zero` together, and the second makes the first unable to fire on the case it is least able to survive: zero is a valid operand, so an uninitialised read succeeds and is indistinguishable from a deliberate zero. `aocl-and-model-build-flags.md` keeps the trap on the argument that a NaN raises SIGFPE at the line that made it rather than reaching every spectral coefficient through the Legendre transform, and that argument does not reach an uninitialised read at all. The pairing is not a decision anyone recorded, and `-finit-real=zero` is meanwhile load-bearing for determinism in a way nothing states, so deleting it is not the move: dropping it leaves garbage rather than detection | `notes/audits/uninitialised-reads-and-implicit-save.md` finding 1 | blocked, and PARKED until the optimisation worktree merges rather than blocked on effort. THE PREMISE THIS ROW WAS DECIDED ON IS CONTRADICTED: it was closed 2026-08-22 on the argument that zero fill is free, since gfortran folds a signalling NaN to a quiet one at -O1 and above so the alternative arms no trap, making the swap a loss on both sides rather than a trade. That argument holds only if the fill itself costs nothing, and the optimisation worktree reports `-finit-real=zero` costing on the order of 30 percent of runtime. If that survives review it is a real trade and the decision has to be retaken on it, so the comment written into `most_compiler_omp` has been REMOVED rather than left standing on a premise now in doubt. THE FILE IS ALSO THE WRONG PLACE NOW: that worktree reworked the build process, so where the flag line lives is itself unsettled. Retake this against the merged build, with the cost measured rather than reported, and do not re-derive it before then [step: rebuild_binaries] |

It adds a dimension to the (resolution, layers, ranks) triple that rule 4 counts binaries over, which is exactly why it goes in the manifest rather than beside it | `notes/audits/uninitialised-reads-and-implicit-save.md` finding 1 | blocked, and PARKED with CLIM-49 until the optimisation worktree merges. Its argument is unaffected and stands: a debug build differs in optimisation level, so its numerics differ and a climatology off it is not comparable to one off the production binary, which is why it is a second binary rather than a flag. Build it against the merged build system [step: rebuild_binaries] |
| CLIM-51 | Read the 103 implicitly SAVEd procedure-body declarations in `plasim/src` and classify each as intended persistent state, harmless write-before-read, or a defect. The thread port tripped exactly one of them and the other 102 have never been examined. Under `-fopenmp` a SAVEd local is SHARED by every thread, demonstrated at seven of eight threads reading a value another thread wrote, while under MPI each rank is a process and its SAVEd locals are private by construction. | CLIM-53 | Settle the offline glacier accelerator: `newsnow.x` and `buildice.x` are built by ExoPlaSim's own `configure.sh` and consume `newdsnow`, `newxsnow`, `lsm` and `persist`, which `glaciermod.f90:610-619` writes at the end of every model call and which every glacier-enabled run this project has made has produced and nobody has ever read. `newsnow` extrapolates a five-year snow tendency forward by a declared interval where snow is shrinking or has persisted a full year, which makes it the only device in the tree for reaching an equilibrium ice sheet inside an affordable run -- the land-ice counterpart of `NCVEG`. Both programs hardcode `NLAT = 32, NLON = 64`, so they are T21 and would silently misread a T42 field, and `buildice`'s uniform 400 m over land is an initialiser for a planet whose ice sheets are already known | `notes/audits/dormant-exoplasim-modules.md` finding 5, `notes/glacier-rough-pass.md` | open, and it is a verdict rather than an implementation: generalise the extrapolator to the resolution ladder and declare what the acceleration is valid for, adopt it as a step in `config/pipeline.yaml` with the model calls it sits between, or state that the four fields are deliberately unconsumed and why. Deleting the loop is a legitimate close. Read with DUST-14, which puts a deposition term on the same ice the accelerator would grow [step: bootstrap_run, baseline_run]. AMENDED 2026-08-22: PALADYN's equilibrium spinup mode is the WORKED EXAMPLE of the verdict this row asks for. It runs vegetation and carbon at an artificial 1000-year internal step, possible only because those components are fully implicit, reaches near equilibrium in about 100 simulated years instead of 10,000, and then states what it is NOT valid for -- processes intrinsically out of equilibrium, naming peatlands and inert permafrost carbon, which need a transient run instead. That is the shape of the answer: not whether the accelerator is correct, but what it is valid for and what has to be integrated honestly. Three devices of this class now sit in reach and one is unrun. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 3c. THIS ROW IS UPSTREAM OF ANY FLOW QUESTION and that ordering is deliberate. `newsnow` grows ice without moving it, which is the cheaper test of whether ice persists for a year at all, and flow is not needed until it does. A shallow-ice model run before PHYS-13's mask exists would be fed a surface mass balance built on orography that evaluates high ground about 7.8 K too warm on average, so it would return zero ice confidently and for the wrong reason. Sequence: PHYS-13, then this row, then whether flow is wanted. A FOURTH DEVICE AND A THIRD IDIOM, 2026-08-22, which bears on what this row's verdict should SAY. cGENIE's GEMlite accelerates by CYCLING -- `gem_notyr` ordinary years, then an accelerated stretch, repeat -- rather than by a single long jump. That is more conservative than PALADYN's 1000-year internal timestep or `newsnow`'s forward extrapolation of a five-year tendency, because the full model periodically re-establishes the state the accelerated component was drifting from, so it is valid under weaker assumptions. Worth knowing before choosing a shape for `newsnow`. AND ALL FOUR STATE THEIR INVALIDITY, which is the property this row is really asking to reproduce: PALADYN's mode excludes processes intrinsically out of equilibrium, GEMlite's cannot handle a transient CO2 pulse because it needs surface-to-atmosphere disequilibrium, and `newsnow` extrapolates only where snow already exists and is shrinking or has persisted. The device is never the deliverable; the statement of what it is valid for is. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 26a. THE GUARD PATTERN FOR AN ACCELERATOR, 2026-08-22, if this one is kept. cGENIE controls its geochemical accelerator by two thresholds and a counter -- engage only when a drift RATE is below `gem_adapt_dpCO2dt`, extend only while an accumulated ERROR stays below `gem_adapt_DpCO2`, and refuse to run unbounded by default with the reason written beside the switch. It also STATES ITS OWN DOMAIN OF VALIDITY in the module header rather than leaving it to be found: GEMlite works on slow equilibration and explicitly fails on a CO2 pulse. Whatever this row settles, an accelerator that ships here should carry both -- a declared validity domain and, if a scalar drift measure exists, the two-sided guard. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 35 |
| CLIM-54 | Decide whether the baseline carries the cyclogenesis index diagnostics, `NSTORMDIAG = 1`, and if so read what this climate's environments say. Potential intensity, the ventilation index, the tropospheric entropy deficit, lower-atmosphere absolute vorticity and the genesis potential index are ENVIRONMENTAL indices, built to be evaluated on coarse fields because no model of this class resolves a storm, so T42 is their intended support rather than a compromise; `hurricaneini` takes `RD` from `gascon` and `absvorticity` uses the model's own `omega`, so the module is already planet-aware where most inherited physics is not. Enabling is more than the switch: codes 322 to 329 have to join `REGULAR_CODES` in `run_exoplasim.py`, pyburn already naming them, and `stormclim` has to be reapplied per segment for the CLIM-17 reason. The thresholds are Komacek et al. (2020)'s Earth calibration and are namelist keys, so a Vesper verdict means what it means only once they are re-derived or explicitly declared | `notes/audits/dormant-exoplasim-modules.md` finding 3 | open. It feeds nothing back, so it rides a segment declared `diagnostic` and costs one namelist key and a postprocessor list rather than a run of its own. The durable half is a finding about whether this world's tropical environments admit cyclogenesis at all, which survives a regeneration; a storm COUNT is not on offer at this resolution and CLIM-55 is why [step: baseline_run] |
| CLIM-55 | Keep the storm-capture half of `hurricanemod` recorded as a declared gap rather than an option. `HC_CAPTURE` hunts a RESOLVED vortex -- 33 m/s in the lower atmosphere with 20.5 m/s at the surface, over at least 30 gridcells, sustained at least 256 timesteps -- and the bar is higher here than on Earth by two compounding factors. A given truncation buys 20% coarser spacing in kilometres because the planet is 1.2 radii (375 km at T42, 188 at T85, 125 at T127, 94 at T170), and the storms are not correspondingly larger: rotation at 0.800 of Earth's raises the deformation radius while 12.81 m/s2 lowers the scale height to 0.766, for a net 0.957. Earth GCMs want roughly 50 km before cyclone-like vortices reach realistic intensity, which is about T320 on this planet | `notes/audits/dormant-exoplasim-modules.md` finding 3 | open as a DECLARED gap on DUST-16's precedent: the process is real, it is not representable at any resolution in the plan, and a storm statistic produced by turning the capture thresholds on anyway would be a property of the grid rather than of the world. It is conditional rather than wrong, which is why it is not wontfix -- the revisit condition is a target support finer than about T320, which neither loop D nor CLIM-52's converter contemplates. Until then this row is why no storm count exists, and why that absence is not a null result [step: baseline_run] |
| CLIM-61 | Price a band-resolved shortwave and longwave against the broadband scheme, on this host, before deciding anything. WHAT THE ROW IS ABOUT, and it is not only accuracy: this scheme carries seven derived per-star corrections onto Lacis-Hansen and Sasamori -- `ozone_scale`, `ozone_uv_weight`, `ozone_visible_weight`, `h2o_sw_weight`, `h2o_sw_level`, `co2_sw_weight`, `cloud_absorption_scale` -- plus a longwave band added for CH4 and N2O. Each is separately derived and citable and none is a knob. The COUNT is the tell, and what it points at is measured: the broadband scheme returns about 40 percent of a line-by-line answer for CO2 and the same for the trace-gas band. Every one of those seven exists because a FITTED broadband absorptance cannot be re-weighted structurally and has to be re-derived, so they are seven manual instances of one operation that a band-resolved scheme performs as configuration. Price the maintenance retired beside the slowdown incurred | `notes/external-model-survey.md` sections 8 and 9, `exoplasim/notes/corrk-cross-check.md`, `exoplasim/notes/trace-gas-band.md` | open and ORDERED after the SHTns forward direction lands, since both touch the same worktree. EXPLORATION: the deliverable is a cost per timestep and a verdict, not a replacement. NOT a defect row -- the shortwave water vapour deficit is one-signed, bracketed and carried under PHYS-9. CANDIDATES, all local. SOCRATES, `references/socrates/`, BSD 3-Clause: `ga7` is 6 shortwave bands with at most 12 k-terms and 9 longwave bands against 2 and 1 here, so about an order of magnitude of radiative work and, with `radstep` over 12 percent of the profile, roughly a twofold whole-model slowdown TO MEASURE. `sbin/Ccorr_k` makes that tunable by dropping absorbers and k-terms this world has no use for. ExoRT, `references/exort/`, is REJECTED on reading rather than second: `n68equiv` covers CO2, H2O and CH4 plus a collision-induced set and carries NO OZONE TABLE AND NO N2O, which discards `ozone_scale`, both derived ozone band weights and CLIM-42's trace-gas band outright, and it costs 68 intervals at 8 Gauss points, about three times `ga7`. Worse on both axes. THE REASON GENERALISES and should be applied to any candidate: ExoRT is built for Archean and hydrogen-rich atmospheres around M dwarfs, and this world's atmosphere is 1 bar of N2, O2 and Ar with 450 ppm CO2 and an ozone layer -- close to modern Earth in COMPOSITION and unlike it only in its STAR. So an Earth-built code with a substitutable stellar spectrum fits this project better than an exoplanet-built code aimed at exotic compositions. RTE+RRTMGP is the remaining alternative. CLIMBER-X was checked and REJECTED under REF-10: two shortwave bands like PlaSim's and a longwave that is a tuned parameterisation over concentrations rather than bands. A CHEAPER RUNG EXISTS AND IS HALF WHAT IT LOOKED LIKE. SPEEDY's shortwave is two bands with fitted absorptivities, the same class as Lacis-Hansen and no gain. Its four longwave bands are worth having because they divide the PLANET's emission and so need no per-star reweighting at all -- but `fband(100:400,4)` is fitted quadratics CLAMPED FLAT below 200 K and above 320 K, which stratospheric temperatures reach in normal operation, so re-deriving them from a Planck integral is part of taking it. THE INTERFACE IS BOUNDED, NOT GUESSED. Isca has already wired SOCRATES into a spectral GCM in 3,452 lines at `references/isca/src/atmos_param/socrates`, with spectral files as namelist strings. It is FMS-based so nothing drops into ExoPlaSim; what transfers is the SPECIFICATION, its call signature naming what a host must supply -- temperature, humidity, ozone, CO2, surface temperature, pressure and height at full and half levels, albedo, zenith cosine, distance factor, cloud fraction, effective radius, cloud water -- and returning heating rates, fluxes, clear-sky counterparts and optionally a spectral outgoing longwave, which would be a new diagnostic here. Its albedo argument is ONE broadband value per column, so a band-resolved atmosphere does not resolve the surface and PHYS-14 stays a separate decision. THE STELLAR INPUT NEEDS NOTHING NEW. `references/socrates/data/solar/trappist1` and ExoRT's spectra are both BT-Settl, the same library and the same three parameters `build_stellar_spectrum.py` already uses, and `examples/trappist1/mk_ga_trappist` is 46 lines that re-weight an existing spectral file against a stellar spectrum WITHOUT regenerating the k-distributions, copying the longwave file unchanged [step: rebuild_binaries]. A FIFTH AXIS, and the candidate is now well-supported without a run. SOCRATES exposes the PLANET as well as the star: `src/modules_core/rad_ccf.F90` declares `planet_radius`, `grav_acc`, `mol_weight_air`, `r_gas_dry`, `cp_air_dry` and `repsilon` as `PROTECTED` rather than `PARAMETER`, and `set_socrates_constants(iu_nml)` reads the first five from a `socrates_constants` namelist under its own comment "Read planet-specific constants from namelist", then DERIVES `repsilon` from `mol_weight_air` so the two cannot drift apart -- the same discipline `config/planet.yaml` uses where mass follows from gravity. It is the only external code read in this survey that gets that structurally right: CLIMBER-X puts Earth's radius, gravity and the Sun's visible share in a compile-time `parameter` block, GOLDSTEIN hardcodes `rsc` and `gsc` inside a subroutine, and Isca uses a namelist for most of it and still let `RADCON` through. For this planet only two of the five move -- radius and gravity -- since 78 percent N2, 21 percent O2 and 0.93 percent Ar is Earth's composition to three figures. WHAT REMAINS GENUINELY OPEN is only the cost per timestep in this model, which is the measurement this row is ordered after the SHTns work to take. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 21 EVIDENCE POINTERS, filed 2026-08-22 during a survey consolidation: the SPEEDY and SOCRATES readings this row rests on are `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 8, and the ExoRT rejection is `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 15.. THE COST IS FAR LOWER THAN THE TOOLING'S SIZE SUGGESTS, 2026-08-22. THE CRUX: the Met Office's own non-solar example, `examples/trappist1/mk_ga_trappist:5-15`, takes the standard Earth `ga9` shortwave file, rewrites BLOCK 2 ONLY with a TRAPPIST-1 spectrum, and runs NO `corr_k` at all -- blocks 3, 5, 10-12 and 17 carried over from Earth verbatim. TRAPPIST-1 is a 2600 K M8 dwarf, a far larger spectral shift than 4965 K, so if the cheap path is accepted there it is accepted here. TWO TIERS: cheap is one `prep_spec` run, seconds; expensive is re-running the correlated-k fit with `+S`, which the tooling's own README puts at "probably several days if not done in parallel" needing about 20 GB, then roughly an hour on re-runs. AND THE LONGWAVE FILE IS ENTIRELY STAR-INDEPENDENT -- its k-fits use Planckian weighting, not stellar -- so an Earth longwave spectral file transfers untouched and half the problem disappears. IF TAKING THE CHEAP TIER, know that THREE star-weighted quantities go stale and not one: block 3's Rayleigh coefficients, block 5's k-term weights, and every cloud and aerosol band average built by `Cscatter_average -S`. The TRAPPIST-1 example regenerates none of them; block 3 and the scatter averages are cheap to redo, block 5 is the expensive tier. THE ATMOSPHERE IS THE EASY PART: the foreign broadener is hard-coded to HITRAN air-broadened half-widths with no substitution mechanism, which is exactly right for a 1 bar N2/O2/Ar column, and the standard p-T grids span 1 Pa to 10 bar and 100 to 400 K. ONE TRAP: refractive-index validity ranges are declared and NEVER ENFORCED -- argon's is 0.140 to 0.568 um and the Sellmeier forms are silently extrapolated, with more K-dwarf flux redward of that bound than solar; the resulting error size is not determined from source. Ozone is fully separable, its own gas index and block entries, standard cross-sections covering 195 to 1100 nm. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 44 |
| CLIM-62 | Cost the two routes to a shallow-ice solver on this project's grids. The claim that a native implementation beats a port is a JUDGEMENT with no measurement behind it, and it should not be inherited. Scope the comparison to the FLOW SOLVER ALONE: neither route supplies the surface mass balance, which is most of the work and is refused on separate grounds, so a comparison that includes it measures the wrong thing. ROUTE A, native on the region mesh: the operator is the doubly nonlinear diffusion `div(D grad H)`, with `D` depending on both `H^(n+2)` and `abs(grad z_S)^(n-1)`. This project already owns an elliptic operator on that mesh with exact closure and a certified uniqueness identity, and it owns an ACTIVE SET, which is what an advancing and retreating margin needs since `H >= 0` is a free boundary. ROUTE B, port MITgcmIS: establish whether the solver is separable from the cubed-sphere geometry and from MITgcm at all, what licence the Zenodo archive carries, and what the grid crossing costs under rule 3. EXPLORATION: the deliverable is a cost for each route and a verdict, not a solver | `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 4 | open, and INDEPENDENT of PHYS-13 and CLIM-53 -- whether this world wants ice is a different question from what a solver costs, and only the second is answerable today. DECLARE THE RULE BEFORE COSTING: the native route wins unless the port is cheaper by a wide margin, because it carries no grid crossing and no external dependency; and if the nonlinear diffusion will not converge on this mesh then route A is unavailable at any cost and the comparison is over. THAT CONVERGENCE IS THE FIRST THING TO TEST AND THE CHEAPEST. GW-9's exponential transmissivity failed on this same mesh under both Picard and Kirchhoff, so a doubly nonlinear diffusion has direct local precedent for failing, and GW-8 measured the discrete operator's own noise floor at about 12 percent relative RMS above l = 1, which bounds what any answer here can mean. Test it against the closed-form similarity solution for shallow ice at zero mass balance, which is an identity with a right answer, rather than against an ice sheet that merely looks plausible [step: any]. Route B is PARTLY ANSWERED already, from the tree now under `references/big-mitgcm/`: `MITgcmIS.py` is 557 lines, imports numpy, scipy, numba and joblib and NOTHING from MITgcm, reading its inputs as binary files instead -- so it is separable from that model. It is NOT separable from the cubed sphere: `read_cs_bin`, `cs_to_vec`, `vec_to_cs` and a `crossmap` face-crossing routine are structural rather than incidental, which is the part rule 3 makes expensive. What remains for this row is the cost, not the separability. A THIRD ROUTE EXISTS AND IS ALREADY ON DISK, AND THE SEPARABILITY QUESTION HAS A PEER ANSWER, 2026-08-22. `references/climber-x/src/ice_sico/` is SICOPOLIS, 27,000 lines, vendored inside CLIMBER-X, and `references/climber-x/src/ice/` is the 1,169-line layer it sits behind -- `ice_init`, `ice_update`, `ice_end`, plus a per-model translation pair. THAT LAYER SERVES TWO DIFFERENT ICE MODELS: it carries `yelmo_to_ice` and `ice_to_yelmo` beside the SICOPOLIS path, and `nml/ice_yelmo_par.nml` configures the second, so the interface is demonstrably model-agnostic rather than fitted to one solver. THIS DOES NOT SHOW MITgcmIS IS SEPARABLE, but it removes the possibility that nothing in this class separates, and it gives the interface a measured size to cost ROUTE B against: roughly 1.2k lines of adapter around a 27k-line solver, twice. It also adds a ROUTE C this row does not consider -- SICOPOLIS or Yelmo behind that same interface -- which should be costed or refused explicitly rather than left unnamed. Route A's argument from the existing elliptic operator and active set is untouched by this. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 4. ROUTE C COSTED, AND IT IS A DOWNGRADE FROM ROUTE A ON THE FREE BOUNDARY, 2026-08-22. SICOPOLIS does NOT solve the `H >= 0` constraint: `calc_thk_m.f90:1306` and `:1376` CLIP, the implicit variants solve the same unconstrained system and clip afterwards, and there is no active set, variational inequality or complementarity condition in the module. The mass the clip destroys or invents is then REBOOKED AS A SURFACE MASS BALANCE CORRECTION at `:1437-1443`, redefining accumulation and runoff after the fact so the diagnostics balance. Route A gets the margin right by construction; Route C gets it approximately and repairs the books. The margin is a per-cell binary against a single-precision epsilon with no sub-grid position. THE GRAVITY EDIT IS SMALL -- 17 lines mention `G`, one parameter defines it, two literals hide it -- SO THE COST IS ELSEWHERE: Earth-tuned dimensional limiters `hd_max = 500` m2/s and `vh_max = 3000` m/a sitting directly on g^3 and g^4 quantities and binding about 2.2x more often here while silently truncating the effect being studied; seven welded Earth region ids with a Greenland exclusion switch and a 604-line discharge scheme documented as Greenland-specific; and no material constant being namelist-configurable, so any change is a whole-model recompile. `hydro_m.f90` is 2,041 lines of DEAD code with a second inconsistent constant set -- do not port it. The coupling surface is NOT the expensive part: 13 fields in, 7 out, about 90 lines of translation, with a 1-based/0-based transpose that is rule 3's class of defect on indices. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 45c-d |
| CLIM-63 | Establish SEMI as the surface mass balance candidate, which is the half CLIM-62 scopes out and is most of the work. MITgcmIS's Positive Degree Day scheme is refused for driving ablation from 2 m air temperature alone, blind to absorbed shortwave over ice, which is the term a K2.5V host changes most. `references/climber-x/src/smb/semi.f90` is the opposite kind of object: it takes surface albedo and downward shortwave in FOUR components each, visible and near-infrared by direct and diffuse, with the derivatives of downward shortwave with respect to albedo, plus cloud, downward longwave, a lapse rate, a zenith cosine and dust. THAT BAND STRUCTURE IS ALREADY THIS PROJECT'S -- the visible and near-infrared split is the decomposition `lib/stellar.py` computes at 0.75 um -- so the per-star reweighting that exists maps onto its inputs instead of being reinvented. It also consumes sub-grid elevation, an elevation standard deviation, slopes and elevation classes and calls `downscaling_mod`, so it runs the balance on a downscaled field rather than a cell mean, and `snow_par` carries `lsnow_dust`, `w_snow_dust` and `dust_con_scale` | `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 3f | open. EXPLORATION: the deliverable is what SEMI requires that this project cannot currently supply, and a verdict on whether the scheme or only its structure is worth taking. ITS INPUTS ARE THE QUESTION and most of the row: a four-component surface albedo and a four-component downward shortwave are more than `radmod.f90` exposes today, and costing that exposure is the work. `smb_bias_corr.f90` is a bias correction against Earth observations and has no meaning here; every constant in the module is a PHYS-class inheritance. ORDERED after PHYS-13 on CLIM-53's argument: a mass balance scheme fed cell-mean orography answers the wrong question however good the scheme is [step: rebuild_binaries]. THE ENTHALPY CHOICE AND ITS COST HERE, 2026-08-22. SICOPOLIS defaults to ENTM, the melting-CTS enthalpy method of Greve and Blatter: the conventional enthalpy solve plus a corrector that re-solves the column from the cold-temperate transition upward so the transition condition holds exactly. THE CORRECTOR MAKES PER-COLUMN COST DEPEND ON HOW MUCH OF THE COLUMN IS TEMPERATE, and with strain heating at g^4 and the pressure-melting gradient 1.31x steeper here, a simulated ice sheet on this world is temperate over more of its volume -- so a run is SLOWER PER TIMESTEP than an Earth run of the same size. Expect that rather than discover it. Sub-shelf melt itself is a two-parameter thermal-forcing law with no gravity; its freezing-point line needs both the gravity rescale above and a salinity decision, and its Earth-hydrography bias corrector should be deleted rather than ported. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 45e |
| CLIM-64 | Price the aerosol indirect effect, which is ABSENT rather than wrong and which no row has ever named. Aerosol here is offline dust, sea salt and volcanic sulfate, each with an optics product, and the clouds are diagnostic: NOTHING connects condensation nuclei to droplet number to cloud albedo. WHAT AN OFFLINE AEROSOL PRODUCT WOULD HAVE TO CARRY IS FOUR NUMBERS PER MODE -- number concentration, median dry radius, geometric standard deviation, and HYGROSCOPICITY KAPPA, which is the only compositional information a condensation solver ever sees; densities, molar masses and dissociation numbers collapse into it before the run starts. That is a far smaller demand than the framing suggests and the existing products could carry it. THE BLOCKER IS THE UPDRAFT: PySDM prescribes vertical velocity and never derives it, there is no buoyancy closure, and an ExoPlaSim gridcell has no resolved updraft, so nothing generates the supersaturation that drives activation. AND THE CHEAP DOOR IS SHUT IN THAT TREE: it implements no Twomey and no Abdul-Razzak-Ghan -- the ARG directory is a reproduction TARGET of digitised figure data. The Koehler algebra is trivial; the missing piece is PEAK SUPERSATURATION, which is exactly what ARG parameterises | `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 50 | open. SCOPE THIS AS AN ACQUISITION FIRST, NOT AN IMPLEMENTATION: price an ARG-class parameterisation that maps aerosol and updraft to droplet number in closed form, and separately decide where an updraft proxy could come from, because without one the chain does not start. NOTE ALSO WHERE THE CHAIN ENDS: PySDM does reach albedo through effective radius, liquid water path, Stephens (1978) optical depth and Bohren (1987) two-stream -- but with NO spectral dependence and NO solar zenith angle, which for a host putting 0.382 of its flux below 0.75 um, and after section 37 priced snow's missing zenith term at 0.30, is one step short of usable rather than the end [step: none]. THE CLOSED FORM EXISTS TWICE OVER, 2026-08-22, and one of them already treats gravity correctly. `pyrcel/legacy/activation.py` is BSD-3, pure numpy plus `scipy.special.erfc`, and carries FOUR schemes side by side -- Abdul-Razzak-Ghan 2000, Morales Barahona and Nenes 2014, Shipway-Abel 2010 and Ming 2006 -- on one set of thermodynamics, which gives this row a second opinion for free. Its inputs are updraft speed, temperature, pressure and per-mode lognormal aerosol with kappa, which is exactly the four-numbers-per-mode set section 50a established and which this project's offline products could carry. Gravity appears once, in the alpha coefficient. SEPARATELY READ `CloudMicrophysics.jl`'s `AerosolActivation.jl`, which is the source of the ARG values PySDM's own test data cites, and WHICH ALREADY CARRIES GRAVITY AS A NAMED PARAMETER rather than a constant -- so it is the reference for where `g` belongs in the derivation, and it additionally corrects peak supersaturation for pre-existing liquid and ice after Korolev and Mazin (2003). Neither needs Earth observations: the ARG fit coefficients are fits to a parcel-model solution, not to data. THE UPDRAFT REMAINS THE BLOCKER and neither source solves it. `notes/external-source-inventory.md` |

| CONV-5 | Make the NetCDF products this project writes declare their own geometry, so a generic reader cannot substitute Earth's without saying so. Three parts. `lat` and `lon` carry `units = "deg"` where CF names the axes `degrees_north` and `degrees_east`, so a reader that does not recognise an axis falls back to guessing it from position. No coordinate system is declared on any of them, so a reader that derives cell areas from one takes an Earth radius: iris `area_weights` uses the radius from the cube's coordinate system when it is present and spherical and falls back to its Earth default only otherwise, which makes a declared spheroid the difference between a right answer and a plausible wrong one. The radius is `planet.radius_earth` in `config/planet.yaml` and is read rather than copied, per rule 2. And the files carry no Gaussian quadrature weight variable, so `ncwa -w` has nothing to find and gives the unweighted mean that is the first of environment.md's three traps. Writing the weight also makes the file the one place the grid's quadrature is RECORDED rather than recomputed, which seventeen modules currently do through `leggauss`; whether those then read it is a separate row and not this one. SCOPE: the writers this project owns, swept together rather than one at a time, since `exoplasim/`, `hydrography/`, `pedology/`, `minerals/` and `aeolian/` all write NetCDF and a partial sweep leaves a reader unable to tell which convention a file follows. NOT `source/`: its conventions are `vendor/orogen/tools/` and `source/README.md`, and a metadata edit there is an overwrite rather than a new build, which rule 7 forbids. NOT the time axis, deliberately -- it stays `units = timesteps` with no calendar, because no CF calendar expresses this orbital period and imposing one would make an Earth-calendar operator succeed silently where it now refuses. The second trap documents a correct artifact rather than a defect in it | `docs/src/reference/environment.md` "Three traps, because these tools assume Earth", `source/README.md` | open; independent of any run and cheap. Products written after the sweep carry the attributes and products written before it do not, which is the tree's resting state rather than a work list [step: bootstrap_climatology, baseline_climatology, analyze_climatology, hydrography, surface_water, groundwater, surface_classes, dust, sea_salt, volcanic_sulfate, prospectivity] |
| CONV-6 | Spike AiiDA as the record of what the pipeline has done, and decide from the answers rather than from the comparison. Install `aiida-core`, `aiida-shell` and `aiida-workgraph` into `.venv`, wrap `hydrography` and `bootstrap_climatology` with `launch_shell_job`, and dry-run one step in the `hours` band. Five questions, all left open by `notes/orchestration-frameworks.md`, and every one about effort or fit rather than about whether AiiDA can do the thing. (1) What does keeping a multi-GB climatology out of the content-addressed store actually cost: does the `remote_folder` route behave as documented, and does a checksum emitted beside the artifact and retrieved on its own give back what the store would have given? `use_symlinks` does not answer this -- it applies to input staging, not output retrieval. (2) Does `aiida-shell` wrap the step scripts as they stand, or must their inputs be exposed as nodes first? (3) Can an Orogen generation sit inside the graph? The exit predicate itself -- carve the intersection of the verdicts taken at the two bounding climates -- is what the `while_`-and-pause pattern is for, so the open part is the generation, which runs a vendored fork and writes the read-only `source/` tree. (4) What does `verdi node delete --dry-run` report against `pipeline.py --purge` on one seed? That is a differential test with a right answer: the two must agree within a pass, and the instance graph should be correct where the `orogen` cut is an approximation. (5) Is a WorkGraph program an acceptable replacement for reading `config/pipeline.yaml`? That one is a preference, settled by looking at both rather than by measuring, and it is the only question here whose answer is a matter of taste. SCOPE: what AiiDA would replace is the record half -- `exoplasim/runs/INDEX.json` as the run registry, `downstream()` and `--purge`, and part of the stamping -- while `config/pipeline.yaml` stays as the declaration, because cost bands and gates are annotations for a person deciding whether to start an expensive step, and the two answer different questions on the same division that keeps `--purge` and `check_consistency.py` apart. This row is NOT a decision to adopt; adoption if taken belongs at a generation boundary, where rule 7 leaves nothing to ingest and the migration cost is therefore about zero | `notes/orchestration-frameworks.md` | open; blocks nothing and nothing waits on it. The broker that the daemon, `submit()` and process kill need is a `rabbitmq` podman container rather than a host package, so the spike installs nothing outside `.venv` [step: hydrography, bootstrap_climatology] |

## FIRE -- fire modelling

10 open of 10 issued.

| id | task | source | status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| FIRE-1 | Add a lightweight ExoPlaSim lightning-potential diagnostic from pre-convective or explicitly upstream CAPE and synchronous convective precipitation, retaining the shared total-flash or discharge-energy precursor needed by ANUT-4 before FIRE-3 reduces it to ground ignitions; define substep accumulation, units and provenance, do not multiply the existing post-`rainstep` code 322 by code 143, and benchmark diagnostic overhead before production activation | `biosphere/notes/fire-model-audit.md` finding 3, `biosphere/notes/abiotic-nutrient-delivery-audit.md` finding 4 | open; the source change and no-simulation checks can proceed independently, but any timing or climate run requires explicit permission [step: baseline_run]. THE CONSUMPTION SIDE IS PRICED AND IT IS ONE SCALAR, 2026-08-22. SPITFIRE reads lightning from an input file whose default is literally named `lightning_fixed`, in `hectare-1 d-1`, and uses it in ONE LINE multiplying by two Earth-tuned constants whose product is 0.008, one being Earth's cloud-to-ground flash fraction. There is NO lightning parameterisation anywhere in LPJmL and the coupled-model path where a live field would arrive is commented out. So what this row produces plugs in unchanged as a per-cell-per-day ignition density -- the architecture is portable and only the field is missing. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 51a. THE HARD INPUT IS ALREADY COMPUTED, 2026-08-22, verified in source: THIS ROW NEEDS AN OUTPUT HOOK, NOT NEW PHYSICS. Both convection schemes in the vendored fork already derive a convective cloud-top level and pressure -- `rainmod.f90:576,596` declares `itop` "cloud top level" and `zptop` "cloud top pressure" and sets them through the Kuo scheme, and `rainmod_bm.f90:424,440` does the same for Betts-Miller. Both are ROUTINE-LOCAL and neither is exported, which is the whole of the gap. THAT MAKES PRICE-RIND DIRECTLY IMPLEMENTABLE and rules out the updraft-mass-flux and ice-flux families, which need diagnostics this model does not carry. The cleanest statement of the scheme is WRF's `module_ltng_cpmpr92z.F`, 101 lines with about 20 lines of physics: `flashrate = 3.44e-5*zkm^4.9/60` over land and `6.57e-6*zkm^4.9/60` over ocean after Michalon (1999), gated on cloud-top temperature below freezing, scaled by cell area. Its inputs are the neutral-buoyancy level, geopotential height there, terrain height, temperature and a land mask -- all available. CARRY THE CALIBRATION CAVEAT: the coefficient and the 4.9 exponent are an Earth fit that cannot be re-derived here, so THE FIELD IS DEFENSIBLE IN SHAPE AND NOT IN ABSOLUTE MAGNITUDE, and what FIRE-3 consumes should be normalised to a declared global flash rate rather than taken as a rate. A ZERO-MODEL-CHANGE FALLBACK if the hook proves awkward: Romps et al. (2014) makes flash rate proportional to CAPE times convective precipitation, and convective precipitation is already an output field. `notes/external-source-inventory.md` |
| FIRE-2 | Consume EFOR's coherent chronological fire-weather sequence -- lightning potential, convective and total precipitation, temperature, humidity, wind, pressure and radiation -- define the fire step against Vesper's 30-hour solar day and 181-day orbit, and conserve integrated fluxes rather than independently smoothing the fields | `biosphere/notes/fire-model-audit.md` finding 5, `biosphere/notes/ecological-climate-forcing-audit.md` findings 4 and 5 | open, builds on EFOR-1 through EFOR-5, BIO-13 and BIO-23; do not infer event order from monthly means [step: baseline_run, lpj_driver] |

| FIRE-4 | Replace SIMFIRE's archive and `cru_ncep` generator-name gate with an explicit fire-driver capability interface that accepts Vesper-native ignition and weather fields and fails closed when any required capability is missing; do not ingest GFED burned-area seasonality or HYDE population | `biosphere/notes/fire-model-audit.md` findings 1 and 2 | open; independent interface work, required before the reduced occurrence model can reach BLAZE [step: lpj_driver, lpj_run] |
| FIRE-5 | Implement a reduced-complexity occurrence-to-area layer at the FIRE-4 seam: INFERNO-like fuel/moisture flammability and a Li-like explicit `fire count * bounded area per fire`, with wind-dependent spread as a declared option and every Earth fuel, humidity, duration, PFT-spread and fire-size coefficient exposed as a prior or bracket rather than a Vesper calibration | `biosphere/notes/fire-model-audit.md` finding 2 | open, blocked on FIRE-2 through FIRE-4; SPITFIRE complexity is deliberately deferred unless this model-form bracket proves inadequate [step: lpj_run] |

AND THE COMPARISON THIS PREFIX HAD NEVER MADE, 2026-08-22: BLAZE IS THE HARDER BLOCKER, NOT THE EASIER ONE. BLAZE has NO ignition model whatsoever -- burned area is a single regression, a biome coefficient times a vegetation-cover power times a Nesterov-maximum power times an exponential in population density, then scaled by an observed monthly burned-area climatology read from a file. The coefficients are fitted to Earth's satellite fire record, the population is HYDE Earth human history, the risk climatology is GFED, and its rate of spread is one line. SPITFIRE by contrast needs ONE INPUT FIELD REPLACED and keeps a mechanistic Rothermel/Albini spread model, four dead fuel classes with class-specific consumption curves, elliptical fire geometry and size-dependent mortality from bark thickness and scorch height. NOTE ONE DISCREPANCY if the two are ever compared numerically: their Nesterov RESET conditions are different quantities -- BLAZE on diurnal temperature range below 4, SPITFIRE on daily minimum below 4 -- while their accumulation terms agree. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 51d |

## GRAV -- gravity

4 open of 10 issued.

| id | task | source | status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GRAV-0 | -- | -- | wontfix, see `archive/tasks.md` |
| GRAV-1 | -- | -- | wontfix, see `archive/tasks.md` |
| GRAV-2 | -- | -- | wontfix, see `archive/tasks.md` |
| GRAV-3 | -- | -- | wontfix, see `archive/tasks.md` |
| GRAV-4 | -- | -- | done, see `archive/tasks.md` |
| GRAV-5 | -- | -- | done, see `archive/tasks.md` |
| GRAV-6 | Glacial erosion carries no gravity term, and Glen's law makes ice velocity go as `g^3` (2.23x here) | `notes/audits/orogen-gravity.md` | blocked on the downscaling pass, deliberately. AMENDED: two further facts make a g^3 term precision on the wrong quantity. `iceFlow` accumulates a CELL TALLY, seeded from the glaciation index and summed downstream, where the hydraulic path was deliberately given `flowSeed = cellArea / refCellKm2` so its accumulation is a real drainage area; the two accumulators disagree about what is resolution-independent, and the same valley collects about four times the ice flow at four times the region count, carving as `iceFlow^0.6`. And per PHYS-13 the index feeding it is an Earth-calibrated latitude threshold blind to this planet's obliquity, spectrum and rotation. Fix the ice mask first. The sub-grid judgement also survives measurement: at four times the region count the mean edge is 7.59 km against a 1-5 km valley, and reaching 1.5 km edges needs about 256 million regions, 26x the run measured. See `notes/audits/orogen-resolution.md`. The audit's recommendation was Option B, carry it as a declared gap: `iceFlow` is a heuristic accumulation rather than a Glen's-law velocity, so a rigorous g^3 on it is precision theatre. Decisive on top of that, a glacial valley is 1-5 km against a 15.19 km mesh cell, so the process is SUB-GRID and cannot be resolved here at any gravity. It belongs with the downscaling machinery, which has to persist sub-grid hypsometry anyway [step: orogen]. AMENDED 2026-08-22, and this does NOT reopen the row: the objection that a `g^3` velocity on an Earth-calibrated ice mask is precision on the wrong quantity is unchanged. What is new is that the `(rho_i g)^n` term has a published reference implementation to take whole rather than a term to bolt on, in BIG-MITgcm's MITgcmIS -- shallow ice, Glen's law at n = 3, Positive Degree Day mass balance, no basal sliding or calving -- and that it costs about 1 CPU-hour per 40,000 years, so ice sheet flow is a SMALL component rather than a large one. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 4. TAKING THE SOLVER IS NOT TAKING THE MODEL, and the split is where the work is. The flow half is a few lines of algebra and the two routes to it, native on the region mesh or a port off the cubed sphere, are costed by CLIM-62 rather than assumed here. The mass balance half is not usable here at all: its ablation is driven by 2 m air temperature alone, with no shortwave, albedo or spectrum, which is structurally blind to the term this world differs on most over ice, and its accumulation assumes densification into glacial ice is instantaneous, which is GRAV-8's missing physics assumed away. So the deliverable from that paper is Eq. 2 to 4, and `A_dot` is the other ninety percent. GRAVITY ENTERS AT FOUR POWERS, NOT ONE, 2026-08-22, from SICOPOLIS's own algebra in `references/climber-x/src/ice_sico/`. The g^3 velocity scaling this row states is CONFIRMED independently: driving stress is g^1, Glen `creep = sigma^2` makes it g^3, so 2.23x. TWO THINGS THIS ROW DOES NOT CARRY. (1) STRAIN HEATING IS g^4, `creep(sigma)*sigma^2`, giving 2.91x -- the thermal feedback is a HIGHER power than the flow, so a simulated ice sheet here goes temperate more readily than the velocity scaling implies, and temperate ice re-enters the flow through the rate factor and the sliding switch. (2) BASAL SLIDING IS ONLY g^(p-q), which is g^1 under the default Weertman exponents p=3 q=2, against g^3 for deformation -- SO THE DEFORMATION-TO-SLIDING RATIO SHIFTS BY g^2 = 1.71x AND THE MODE OF FLOW CHANGES, not just its speed. `c_slide` is dimensional, `m/(a*Pa^(p-q))`, so its Earth-tuned value is wrong here by exactly that factor. AND TWO EARTH GRAVITIES HIDE IN PER-METRE COEFFICIENTS WITH NO `G` ON THE LINE, failure-mode class 32 in its unit form: `BETA = 8.7d-04 K/m`, the Clausius-Clapeyron gradient per metre OF ICE, is `dTm/dp` times `rho_i*g` and must become 1.136e-3 here, propagating into the pressure-melting point, the cold-temperate transition position and temperate-layer drainage; and the sub-shelf freezing point's 7.64e-4 per metre is the same shape, becoming 9.98e-4. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 45a-b. AND A CHEAP ISOSTASY IMPLEMENTATION WITH A NON-OBVIOUS SIGN, 2026-08-22 -- adjacent to this row rather than answering it, since Landlab carries no glacial erosion law. Its `flexure` component is not a plate PDE but a superposition of analytic point-load Green's functions, with Airy as a one-line short-circuit. Gravity enters at exactly ONE place, the mantle specific weight, at power 1. What propagates is worth stating because the sign surprises: flexural parameter goes as `g^-1/4` (0.935x here), deflection per unit LOAD IN NEWTONS as `g^-1/2` (0.875x), but **deflection per unit ice or sediment THICKNESS as `g^+1/2`, 1.143x** -- because a load is a weight, so heavier gravity makes a given thickness depress the bed MORE even though response per newton falls. Airy cancels gravity exactly. Two defects before copying: the 1-D class omits the cell-area factor the 2-D path applies, so its result is off by the grid spacing, and the gFlex wrapper's step method calls a name that does not exist. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 53c. IT IS COMPLETELY SEPARABLE: it reads per-cell scalars and writes erosion rates. AND THE GRAVITY ENTRY POINT IS EXPLICIT AND LOAD-BEARING: line 1416 sets `pe = 0.01*te` with the comment recording that `te` is normalised by `rho_ice*g`, so that 0.01 IS EARTH'S GRAVITY BAKED INTO A CONSTANT and becomes 0.01306 here. With `pe^3` in the quarrying law, **gravity enters quarrying as g^3 INDEPENDENTLY of the g^3 already priced through Glen's law** -- which is precisely the term this row says is absent. The real cost is not the law but its second input: effective pressure needs a subglacial hydrology assumption, where sliding speed comes from the ice dynamics already held. Two solvers died there; do not re-run it. The form to try is saturated thickness, `T = K (h - z_bottom)`, the textbook unconfined case the constant `D` currently approximates as confined. It is depth-selective, so it carries the governor; it is LINEAR in head, so cell-averaging is exact and there is no Jensen term; and it is Boussinesq, which under the Kirchhoff substitution `u = (h - z_bottom)^2 / 2` is exactly linear for uniform `K` and `z_bottom`, so the fixed point that killed the exponential does not arise. The `h <= z` box constraint survives the substitution monotonically, as `u <= (z - z_bottom)^2 / 2`, so the complementarity structure is kept [step: hydrography] |

(1) A FACTOR OF TWO AND ONE CONSTANT FOR BOTH FLUXES: `f_sat = min(f_max*exp(-f_over/2*z), 1)` while `R_ss = R_sb*exp(-f_over*z)`, so the surface decay is HALF the subsurface decay and a single calibrated constant sets both -- bracketing it moves surface and subsurface runoff together, which the form as stated here does not show. (2) The `min(...,1)` cap is explicit. (3) PARAMETER PROVENANCE SPLITS EXACTLY THE WAY THIS ROW WANTS: `f_over` and `R_sb` are labelled "calibrated" in their docstrings, while `f_max` is "computed from the topographic index CDF per grid cell" -- terrain-derived, and computable here from the region mesh -- which independently supports declaring and bracketing `f_grad` rather than fitting it. (4) ICE: the saturated column is computed TWICE, including ice for the surface saturated fraction and liquid-only for the subsurface flux, so frozen ground makes saturation-excess surface runoff but no subsurface flow. Neither this row nor PALADYN's form carries that, and it matters in a cold winter hemisphere. ON THE DECISION THIS ROW FLAGS: ClimaLand also estimates water table depth from column water content rather than a lateral solve, so the column estimator is the field's normal practice and the Dupuit-Forchheimer solution is the departure that needs the argument. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 36. A THIRD CONSUMER OF THIS INDEX, WITH A CALIBRATION REQUIREMENT, 2026-08-22. CLIMBER-X's three wetland-extent schemes at `src/lnd/surface_hydro.f90:565-613` -- SIMTOP, DYPTOP and a CTI-CDF lookup -- all key off a per-gridcell compound topographic index, after PALADYN and ClimaLand's SIMTOP. THE CONSTRAINT THAT RIDES ALONG: `cti_mean_crit = 5.5` is an absolute threshold below which no wetland forms, so this row's index needs an absolute calibration and not merely a consistent ranking, and the score this row demands has to test the index on that footing. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 42d |

THE RETENTION CLOSURE IS A FAMILY CHOICE THIS CONTRACT LEAVES IMPLICIT, 2026-08-22. `references/climaland/src/standalone/Soil/retention_models.jl` declares `AbstractSoilHydrologyClosure` with `vanGenuchten` and `BrooksCorey` as its two implementations, and parameterises `RichardsParameters` over the closure type so the choice is made at construction. This project sits entirely in ONE family without having recorded that a family was chosen: LPJ-GUESS's Cosby (1984) derivation is a Clapp-Hornberger form and PALADYN uses Clapp-Hornberger too. The two differ most in their tails -- Brooks-Corey has a discontinuous air-entry point and a power-law tail, van Genuchten is smooth through saturation -- which matters here specifically, because a quarter of this land is playa clastic, so wetting and drying through near-saturation is a common state rather than an edge case. That rests on the PLAYA FRACTION -- which LITH-27 establishes is itself derived from endorheism, since Orogen assigns basin fill on `!!c.endorheic`, so it carries the same pre-carve limit and moves with the carve list. The argument is therefore about a class of surface this terrain produces, not about a settled area, and it survives only in that weaker form until LITH-27 reports what the derived fraction is. NOT an argument for switching: an argument that this contract should carry the closure as a NAMED property with its family stated, so a consumer knows which it is reading and a later comparison has something to vary. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 23b |

Read it for STRUCTURE only: ClimaLand is CMIP-class and GPU-oriented, so it is not a candidate for adoption, and PALADYN stays the EMIC-class implementation reference because it runs at a comparable cost. `notes/external-model-survey.md` sections 23a and 23c. A PEER IMPLEMENTATION OF THE SAME ABSTRACTION DISAGREES BY AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE, 2026-08-22, and this project's side is the permissive extreme. Both ExoPlaSim and cGENIE's ENTS scale land evaporation by a soil-moisture factor and differ in that factor: `landmod.f90:418` is LINEAR, `min(1, W/(drhsfull*Wmax))` with `drhsfull = 0.4` at `:53`, so it evaporates at the POTENTIAL RATE for any bucket above 40 percent full; `surflux.F:1334` is QUARTIC, `min(1, (W/bcap)**4)`, reaching potential only at a full bucket. At equal fractional fill the ratio runs 39x at 0.4, 16x at 0.5, 8x at 0.6 and 2x at 0.8, agreeing only when full. Compare the SHAPE only: capacity differs too, ExoPlaSim's `wsmax` being a uniform scalar against ENTS's carbon-dependent `bcap`, and ENTS's `k8`/`k9`/`k10` arrive by namelist and are NOT shipped in the vendored tree, so the capacity half cannot be closed from here. Neither limiter is right and this is a bracket, not an adoption -- but the replacement column this row specifies should carry the limiter as a DECLARED choice with the bracket beside it rather than inheriting the linear form silently. The direction reaches the carve: at a given bucket state the permissive form sends more precipitation to evaporation and less to runoff, and `scripts/error_budget.py` already records E/R as amplified into the carve. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 32a. AND THE LIMITER HAS A SHAPE VOCABULARY PLUS A SCOPE WARNING, 2026-08-22. ClimaLand's `src/standalone/Vegetation/soil_moisture_stress.jl` carries `beta = min(1, max((theta-theta_low)/(theta_high-theta_low),0)**c)`, cited to Egea et al. (2011), `references/egea2011-water-stress-in-coupled-photosynthesis-conductance-models.pdf`. THE THREE PARAMETERS ARE THE RIGHT AXES for the replacement column's limiter and both behaviours above sit on them -- ExoPlaSim at `c = 1` with the knee at 0.4 of capacity, ENTS at `c = 4` at full capacity -- so implementing the general form makes the bracket a runtime selection rather than a code fork. DO NOT CITE EGEA FOR IT: that paper is about water stress in coupled photosynthesis-stomatal conductance models and its beta multiplies PHOTOSYNTHESIS, applied to `An` in `photosynthesis_farquhar.jl:335` and deliberately not re-applied in stomatal conductance, evaluated on ROOT-ZONE AVERAGED moisture weighted by root distribution. ExoPlaSim's `drhs` and ENTS's `beta` multiply the SURFACE EVAPORATION FLUX over vegetated and bare ground alike from a store with no depth. Same algebra, different flux. THE THIRD AXIS SURVIVES THAT CORRECTION AND IS THE USEFUL ONE: `theta_low` is the wilting point or residual water fraction, and both models here pin it at zero, so each approaches zero flux only as its store approaches empty and water held below the wilting point is still available to be removed. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 33 |

A SPECTRAL TERM THE SLAB HIDES, 2026-08-22: water's e-folding depth never exceeds 0.51 m anywhere above 0.75 um, and 61.8 percent of this star's flux sits there against 46.3 percent of the Sun's, so a larger share of the shortwave is deposited in the topmost water as heat. A one-layer slab cannot see this. A multi-layer column can, and its penetrative solar heating would be MORE surface-concentrated than Earth tuning assumes, stratifying the top of the column more strongly -- so this belongs in the case for the column rather than being discovered after it. OCN-10 already names net and penetrative solar in its forcing contract. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 40e |

Use a zero-global-integral ocean dipole; bind grid, calendar, interval, units, source hash and land/sea/ice mask; require the reported accumulation to reproduce the written field and its area-weighted global integral to remain zero | `notes/audits/ocean-and-marine-biosphere.md` finding 2 and section 8a | open, and instrumentation rather than physics, so it is a diagnostic segment and not an orbit.
