AMENDED 2026-08-22, and CORRECTED: an earlier version of this amendment claimed a three-dimensional ocean costs about four times, from BIG-MITgcm's 300 CPU-hours per 100 simulated years against this stack's 76. That ratio is a COUPLED SYSTEM against a BARE ATMOSPHERE and is not the price of an ocean; it is confounded on the atmosphere side too, since SPEEDY at five levels is much cheaper than ExoPlaSim at T42 with ten. What survives is narrower: BIG-MITgcm's own breakdown makes its 300 almost entirely the coupled fast system, so it is an atmosphere-plus-ocean figure against an atmosphere-plus-slab one, which bounds nothing tightly but is a real comparison. Report this row's cost in wall clock AND in whatever unit the coupling cadence makes meaningful, because CPU-hours per simulated year assumes every component advances on one clock and this pipeline is asynchronous: cost per PASS is the number that decides affordability at loop A's cadence, not cost per simulated year. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 2. THE TREE IS NOW LOCAL, vendored at `vendor/cgenie/` as a subtree from `derpycode/cgenie.muffin` master, MIT, so this row is workable rather than blocked on an acquisition. Two of its questions are already answered by the vendoring. WHETHER IT BUILDS HERE: not as it stands -- `genie-main/user.mak` sets `GENIE_ROOT = $(HOME)/cgenie.muffin` and `RUNTIME_ROOT = ../../cgenie.muffin`, so repointing those is the first fork change. LICENCE AND PROVENANCE: MIT, and the commit is a subtree merge in this repository's history rather than the state of a directory outside it. What remains is the measured cost and the Vesper-parameter question, which is the whole point of the row. NOTHING HERE IS AN ADOPTION: `vendor/cgenie` has no consumer, no pipeline row and no step, and it stays that way until this row returns a verdict. THE VESPER-PARAMETER QUESTION HAS A PARTIAL ANSWER, from reading the tree 2026-08-22: calendar and rotation are consumed, radius and gravity are NOT. `ini_gold_nml` carries `sodaylen`, `sidaylen`, `yearlen`, `nyear` and the depth scale, while `initialise_goldstein.F` hardcodes Earth's radius and gravity into the non-dimensionalisation and four derived scales carry them. Rotation is parameterised but fails open to Earth's rate when the two day lengths are set equal. OCN-12 owns the inventory; what this row takes from it is that the answer is not binary and the radius and gravity half is source work rather than configuration. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 10e. AN ORDERING FALLS OUT OF THE VALIDATION SUITE and belongs here because this row owns the parameter change. `genie-knowngood/`'s GOLDSTEIN references are overturning streamfunctions reported through `opsisc`, which carries Earth's hardcoded `rsc`, so every number in the suite is a number at Earth's parameters. Moving radius and gravity for this planet destroys the suite's applicability as its first act. Either let OCN-19 and OCN-20 land first, since they are correctness-preserving changes at fixed physics and the suite is exactly what they need, or DECLARE a replacement acceptance test before the parameters move -- and it cannot be a comparison against those files. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 13c. THE SPIN-UP ARGUMENT WAS PROBED AND SURVIVES, 2026-08-22. cGENIE ships `genie-gemlite` beside `goldlite` and `ocnlite`, which looked like it might undercut the offline choice. It does not: `genie.F` cycles it as `mod(koverall, kgemlite*kocn_loop)`, running `gem_notyr` ordinary years and then entering a GEM cycle that copies tracer arrays AND climate state -- the comment says "here: sea-ice" -- into GEMlite and holds them. It is offline-tracer acceleration for carbonate chemistry once the circulation is settled, and does nothing for the circulation itself. Its own header also records that it cannot handle a CO2 pulse, since it needs ocean-surface to atmosphere disequilibrium to work. So the claim that equilibrating a deep ocean costs thousands of model years stands unmodified. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 26. THE COST MECHANISM IS IN THE TREE, 2026-08-22. `genie-plasim/src/fortran/plasim.f90:604-655` implements PlaSim-GOLDSTEIN gearing: with `ngear = 1` the atmosphere integrates for `ngear_years_plasim` years while accumulating daily means, then RETURNS IMMEDIATELY for the next `ngear_multiple - 1` blocks while the ocean is driven from the stored seasonal cycle, so the atmosphere runs one block in `ngear_multiple`. That is what makes a coupled ocean affordable and it should enter this row's cost model, not be rediscovered after it. The non-obvious part: the SST-dependent terms are recomputed live against `tstar_ocn` every geared step -- latent via saturation specific humidity, net longwave, sensible -- and only the coefficients and downward fields are replayed, because naive replay of net heat flux severs the feedback that holds SST. The source comment says "Needed for stability". Evaporation is deliberately not recomputed, for moisture conservation. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 30c. ACCELERATOR INVENTORY, 2026-08-22: cost here is not one mechanism but two, controlled differently. Gearing skips the ATMOSPHERE on a fixed schedule with no adaptive guard, `ngear_multiple` being a plain integer. GEMlite skips the BIOLOGY AND CIRCULATION for geochemistry with the two-sided adaptive guard in section 35b. Price them separately; they compose, and only the second bounds its own error. Note also that GEMlite keeps a SECOND live copy of the ocean state -- its header says "there is an entirely seperate `ocn` for BIOGEM" -- which is the duplicate-state hazard rule 5 exists for and would need a provenance answer before adoption. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 35PORTING COST IS ASYMMETRIC AND SHOULD BE PRICED THAT WAY, 2026-08-22: the geochemistry is namelist-configurable while the calendar and the physical scaling constants are compile-time `PARAMETER`s stated in more than one place, so adoption cost sits in the physics core rather than spread evenly. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 40. A PER-ITERATION ADOPTION COST THIS ROW SHOULD CARRY, 2026-08-22. `genie-paleo` is 286 pre-baked Earth geographies and no code, and the cost for a NEW geography is not the bathymetry -- that regrids -- it is the `.psiles` and `.paths` pair, which is TOPOLOGICAL: an integer island-label map and a HAND-ORDERED list of (direction, i, j) triples tracing a closed integration circuit around each island for the barotropic streamfunction solve. These cannot be produced by regridding a heightfield; muffingen generates them interactively. SO IT IS MANUAL WORK PER BUILD, AND IT INVALIDATES ON EVERY GENERATION THAT CHANGES CONTINENT CONNECTIVITY -- which is what a new Orogen generation does. Price it per iteration of loop A, not once. Also note the tuning infrastructure is inert here: three `errfn_*` programs and a HadCM3 target file compile to binaries that score the model against observed phosphate and alkalinity, and this world has nothing to tune against. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 46d |
| OCN-4 | Scope ECOGEM as the ecosystem tier, with driver-agnostic MARBL as the named fallback: licence/version/provenance; tracer and trait-count cost; restart and diagnostic state; required C-N-P-S-Fe-Si, alkalinity/DIC/O2 and optical inputs; absolute-time, K-star photon, gravity/sinking and temperature dependencies; what the covarying trait space must DECLARE; and every fixed Earth relation or inventory that survives. Darwin leaves the board with MITgcm, being an MITgcm package, and this costs nothing on the axis that mattered: trait-based ecology was never the discriminator between them, since both make community composition emerge from a declared trait space. Compare ECOGEM against MARBL on interface burden and on what each supplies as fixed Earth types. EXPLORATION: the deliverable is a scoping note | `notes/external-model-survey.md` sections 12, 16 and 40d, `notes/audits/ocean-and-marine-biosphere.md` | open, and BOTH CANDIDATES ARE NOW LOCAL AND READ: ECOGEM inside `vendor/cgenie/genie-ecogem` from the vendoring, MARBL at `references/marbl/`, BSD-style from UCAR and in references rather than vendor because this row's deliverable is a scoping note. THE COMPARISON SPLITS SCIENTIFIC AGAINST ENGINEERING and should not be forced to a winner. ECOGEM WINS THE ECOLOGY, on the mechanism this row names: the cost is a FILE SWAP, verified at source: `par_ecogem_plankton_file` is a namelist string, `sub_init_populations` counts data lines and sets `npmax` from them, and every plankton-dimensioned array is ALLOCATABLE, so the ladder runs 1 / 8 / 9 / 11 / 16 / 18 / 64 populations across nine shipped `.eco` files with no recompilation. Sixteen is the reference configuration and not the price this row once quoted. TWO CORRECTIONS TO WHAT THAT LADDER MEANS: the 0.6 to 1900 um log spectrum is `8P8Z`'s, not the ladder's -- `32P32Z.eco` is a different grid, phyto 0.10 to 1224 and zoo 0.15 to 2800 with the offset not mirrored, so the sixty-four rung changes the size RANGE and not only the count; and the `3Diat4ZP_PiEu` against `NoDiat4ZP_PiEu` pair is not a free on/off pre-registration, since the diatom file carries 11 rows against 8, so it is a guild ADDED rather than a switch thrown. THE TRAIT SPACE THE ROW ASKS ABOUT IS NOT THE DATA FILE: it is 36 allometric `_a`/`_b` coefficient pairs in `ini_ecogem_nml`, applied as 39 `coef_a * volume ** coef_b` statements, so a population is a size and the traits follow. MARBL is the structural opposite and NOT because its count is locked -- `PFT_defaults` accepts `user-specified` and the shipped `+cocco` variant moves the count -- but because it has no allometric generator at all: 38 hand-set parameters per autotroph with a poison default of `1e34`. That, and not the file swap, is why ECOGEM wins the ecology. MARBL WINS THE ENGINEERING, and also not closely: regression tests, baselines, build tests, `code_consistency.py`, YAML settings with a JSON form carrying per-configuration defaults, declared dependencies, units and datatypes -- AND A STANDALONE DRIVER -- which is a TEST HARNESS and not an offline model: `marbl.F90` dispatches ten single-shot test names and `call_compute_subroutines` calls the compute routines once per column, with no time loop and no tracer update. It can answer a tendency-sensitivity question and cannot produce a spun-up state. ECOGEM has no entry in `genie-knowngood`, no test suite, no settings schema and no driver of any kind. STOICHIOMETRY IS NOT A WASH, and the earlier reading missed where ECOGEM keeps its: BIOGEM exposes `par_bio_red_POP_PON` and others in `ini_biogem_nml` with `bio_part_red` four-dimensional, but ECOGEM buries four Redfield literals in its own BIOGEM coupling arithmetic -- 138/106, two 16.0 and 6.625 -- none of them namelist-reachable. MARBL exposes three and derives seven from a fixed chain. THE PHOTON AXIS INVERTS TOO: MARBL's `f_qsw_par = 0.45_r8` is a Fortran `parameter` with no YAML entry and its six attenuation literals are hardcoded, while ECOGEM's `PARfrac`, `k_w` and `k_chl` are all namelist, so ECOGEM is the one correctable from a configuration file. A SECOND BURIED SOLAR CONSTANT, and it is this project's own recurring defect: `ecogem_box.f90:298` converts light as `E0 = PARlocal/0.2174`, which is 4.5998 umol/J and is the marine twin of the fixed 550 nm Earth coefficient BIO-25 found in LPJ photosynthesis. Read the two together. WHAT TO ACT ON WITHOUT DECIDING, restated now the driver is understood: what MARBL can be made to answer before an ocean host exists is a TENDENCY at a stated state, not a candidate, so the sequencing advantage is real but much narrower than it read. AND THE TWO ARE NOT INDEPENDENT CHOICES: ECOGEM cannot be extracted from cGENIE, so if OCN-3 selects cGENIE it is nearly free, and if OCN-3 does not, ECOGEM is unavailable at any price and MARBL's true cost is the tracer transport host underneath it. `notes/external-model-survey.md` sections 12, 13a and 16. A SPECTRAL PRECONDITION FOR ANY ECOSYSTEM TIER, 2026-08-22, which applies to ECOGEM and MARBL alike and not only to BIOGEM: marine light limitation is specified as an irradiance half-saturation in W m-2 and a SINGLE SCALAR light e-folding depth in metres, both solar calibrations. Water is transparent in the blue-green and strongly absorbing in the red and near-infrared, so a K dwarf's redder flux gives a SHALLOWER photic zone and production confined nearer the surface. The direction follows from the spectrum alone; the magnitude needs `k25v_hr.dat` integrated against water absorption, which is a desk calculation and should precede tier selection rather than follow it, since it changes the vertical structure any tier resolves. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 40d. MAGNITUDE COMPUTED, 0.712, from artifacts already in the tree and with no new number. `exoplasim/data/water/hale_querry_1973_liquid_water.dat` gives water's absorption above 0.75 um, and the LONGEST e-folding depth anywhere in that range is 0.51 m, so all of shortwave band 2 is deposited in the top metre or two as heat and what reaches the photic zone is band 1 and nothing else. Band-1 flux fraction is 0.382 for k25v -- reproducing the project's canonical `flux_fraction_band1` exactly -- against 0.537 for a 5772 K Planck, which is `analysis/vegetation_albedo.py`'s own solar reference. THIS STAR DELIVERS 71 PERCENT AS MUCH LIGHT BELOW THE SURFACE LAYER. Limits, none of which change the sign: it is the flux reaching below the surface layer and not the photic DEPTH, which additionally needs visible attenuation that is not extracted here; and it is pure water, where productive water's chlorophyll and CDOM absorb more in the blue and partly offset it. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 40e EVIDENCE POINTER, filed 2026-08-22: the comparison this row rests on is now `notes/audits/ecosystem-tier-ecogem-marbl.md`, read at source and carrying its own limits section; `notes/external-model-survey.md` sections 12c and 16 are the earlier reading and have been corrected in place where that note contradicted them. WHAT REMAINS BEFORE THIS CLOSES is the SILICON axis and nothing else: the row asks for required C-N-P-S-Fe-Si inputs, and `squota` and the silicifier path were traced structurally only. `references/INDEX.md:516` records Naidoo-Bagwell et al. (2024), the EcoGEnIE 1.1 diatom extension, as `held` rather than read, and the note states that any silicon comparison between the tiers needs it first. |
| OCN-5 | DECLARE the forcing procedure, provenance chain and exit predicate for the ocean-transport loop before its first pass, and add the loop and its OCN-2/OCN-10/OCN-11 steps to `config/pipeline.yaml` in the same commit. An ocean model forced by a climatology a zero-transport slab produced, and restored toward that climatology's own sea-surface temperature, returns a transport of zero by construction. Use flux forcing with no temperature restoring, retain weak salinity restoring only as an explicit control, and converge heat transport, SST/ice, global energy, freshwater/salt closure and the climate fields that drive the carve verdict | `notes/audits/ocean-and-marine-biosphere.md` sections 7, 8a and 8f | open, and a DECISION rather than a measurement, which is why it is a task and the run is not. Ocean physics is inside loop A because transport can move design flux and the carve verdict; SPAT-8 must replay it at every candidate support. Ocean biology stays outside loop A while OCN-16 is closed, and opening carbon feedback creates a separate declared loop rather than silently extending this one. THE CANDIDATE SATISFIES HALF OF THIS ROW BY CONSTRUCTION AND CANNOT SATISFY THE OTHER HALF, 2026-08-22. cGENIE has NO sea-surface temperature or salinity restoring: `surf_ocn_sic.F` computes surface flux terms, `fx0o`, `evap`, `fxsen` and the sea-ice counterparts, and the ocean takes fluxes from whichever atmosphere it is coupled to. The one name that looks like restoring is not -- `rel` in `common /ocn_relax/` is used at `velc.f:103` as `u = rel*u1 + (1-rel)*u`, a numerical under-relaxation between solver iterates, so record it as a solver control lest somebody retire it as physics. The genuine explicit control is `get_hosing.F`, freshwater forcing with `hosing`, `hosing_trend` and `nyears_hosing` in `ini_gold_nml`, called UNCONDITIONALLY from `goldstein.F:259` and added to the freshwater flux at line 297 -- always in the path, inert only by parameter value, which is why a zero default is not the same as an absent term and it belongs in the provenance chain. THE SPLIT: this row's warning joins two failures with an "and" -- forced by a slab-derived climatology AND restored toward its SST. The candidate rules out the second by construction. Nothing in it rules out the first, because that defect is in what this project SUPPLIES. So read the requirement as two with different owners: no restoring is checkable once against cGENIE; flux forcing that does not encode the answer is this project's and is what the declared procedure is actually for. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 27. THE MECHANISM, DERIVED 2026-08-22, and it is sharper than this row's own statement. cGENIE tunes its atmosphere to make room for its ocean: `tstipa.f` moves heat and moisture as `betaz(l)*uatm(1,i,j)` and `betam(l)*uatm(2,i,j)` plus `diffa`, and `ini_embm_nml` exposes `diffamp(2)`, `diffwid`, `difflin`, `betaz(2)` and `betam(2)`, so the atmosphere-ocean transport PARTITION is a tuned quantity there. THIS PROJECT CANNOT DO THAT. On a slab with `nhdiff` off the ocean transports nothing, so ExoPlaSim's atmosphere carries the entire poleward load through resolved dynamics, with no coefficient to turn down. Hand those fluxes to a dynamic ocean and the total becomes an atmosphere already carrying everything plus an ocean carrying more. THE FLUX-FORCED FAILURE IS THE OPPOSITE SIGN FROM THE RESTORED ONE this row describes: not zero transport, but OVER-transport, because the flux field was computed under the assumption that the ocean does nothing. THIS APPLIES TO THE ARCHITECTURE OCN-10 SPECIFIES AND NOT TO THE PUBLISHED PATH, which keeps EMBM and lets it compute the heat fluxes with a tunable transport -- see OCN-10's fork. Under the no-EMBM architecture the loop is necessary rather than stylistic -- a single pass cannot converge the partition, because the atmosphere in the forcing climatology has not been told the ocean exists. And it is why OCN-2 comes first: the return path is `nfluko = 1` reading surface code 903 under the comment "prescribed advection", and verifying that channel with a known field is cheaper than discovering it is wrong once a circulation exists to blame. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 28. A PEER EXIT PREDICATE WORTH COPYING, TWO-SIDED, 2026-08-22. cGENIE's GEMlite accelerator is controlled in `genie-main/genie.F` by ENTRY ON A RATE and EXTENSION ON AN ERROR INTEGRAL: `:621-638` extends the full-model phase whenever the year-on-year pCO2 change exceeds `gem_adapt_dpCO2dt`, default 0.1 ppm/yr, so the accelerator never engages while the system is moving fast; `:808-823` extends the accelerated phase only while cumulative drift from its starting pCO2 stays under `gem_adapt_DpCO2`, default 0.1 ppm. Unbounded extension is off by default with the reason recorded at the switch, and `gem_yr_min`/`gem_yr_max` bound the phase at 10 and 1000. THE PRECONDITION THAT MAKES IT WRITABLE is a single scalar summarising the accelerated subsystem's drift that is already computed; name that number for the ocean-transport loop when declaring this row's exit predicate, because without one the predicate can only be a fixed schedule. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 35 |
| OCN-6 | Establish what spectrally resolved shortwave penetration into the modelled ocean would cost as code, and what it is worth once there are layers to put it in. `mksst` adds the whole atmospheric heat flux to level 1 and lower levels are reached only by `vdiffkl`; compare a clear-water K-star attenuation profile with OCN-14 pigment/particle brackets, require column energy closure and exact recovery of the surface-deposition reduction, and keep biological absorption separate from OCN-7's reflected fraction. THE SPECIFIC SCALARS ARE NAMED, 2026-08-22, if ECOGEM is selected: `k_w` for water and `k_chl` for chlorophyll, both scalars in `ini_ecogem_nml`, so the sub-surface light field is spectrally flat and a clear-water K-star attenuation profile has nothing to attach to. THE ERROR IS ONE-SIGNED, which this row can use: a solar-tuned `k_w` under a redder star OVERSTATES the euphotic depth, because water absorbs strongly beyond about 600 nm and this star puts more of its photosynthetically useful flux there. So the bound is a floor on the correction rather than a bracket around it. The present 0.014 to 0.28 K price is explicitly DECLARED and suggests this does not earn its place alone; it must not become a central pathway until its optical span is sourced and OCN-14 supplies a modelled state. The interface remains cheap if another reason later requires spatial ocean albedo [step: surface_albedo]. THE SPECTRAL HALF IS ANSWERED, NEGATIVELY, 2026-08-22. `exoplasim/surfacespecs.py`'s `oceanblend` weighted against a 5772 K Planck gives a broadband 0.070, reproducing `doceanalb = 0.069` and confirming it as the provenance; weighted against `k25v` it gives 0.068. Water's reflectance is low and nearly flat across the 0.75 um split, 0.076 against 0.065, so a K-star reweighting of the ocean albedo is worth 0.002 and does not earn its place -- which is the same verdict this row already reached from the temperature side, now reached independently from the spectrum. WHAT REMAINS IS THE SPATIAL HALF ONLY, and the two-band interface question, since `doceanalb(1:2)` is a band array holding one value in both elements like every other surface in the model. PHYS-14 owns that defect class; this row keeps the spatial variation. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 11c. THIS ROW'S PREMISE IS PARTLY WRONG AND ITS INSTRUMENT ALREADY EXISTS, 2026-08-22. `seamod.f90:155-158` does apply two namelist scalars, and then `radmod.f90:2895-2904` OVERWRITES the open-ocean part with a zenith-dependent formula, so in the default configuration those scalars survive only for the land and sea-ice fractions and `doceanalb` contributes nothing to open water. THERE ARE THREE BRANCHES, by `necham` and `necham6`: the default `necham=1` gives ECHAM-3, `min(0.05/(zmu0+0.15), 0.15)`; `necham6=1` gives Briegleb, which is the same formula cGENIE integrates in `ocean_alb.F`; both zero gives the constant. SO THE SPATIAL VARIATION THIS ROW ASKS TO COST IS ALREADY PRESENT AND ZENITH-DRIVEN, and what is available is a free A/B between two shipped parameterisations. Flux-weighted daily means: they agree within about ten percent at high sun -- 0.058 against 0.052 at the equator, 0.075 against 0.079 at 45 degrees equinox -- and diverge to 1.64x at 45 degrees solstice and 1.82x at 60 degrees winter, because ECHAM-3's cap binds above 79.5 degrees zenith and Briegleb has no ceiling. THE DIVERGENCE REGION IS LARGER HERE: at 32 degrees obliquity against Earth's 23.4 more of the year sits in the low-sun regime, and that is the winter hemisphere where sea ice forms. Both zenith branches are spectrally flat, which the source states in a comment rather than denying, so the negative spectral result above is unaffected. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 29 |

This project sets `two_band_albedo: true` specifically to obtain spectral surface albedo, and `solarini` duly computes 0.0744 and 0.0636 for the two ocean bands; over ice-free ocean, which is most of the planet, that is undone every radiation step for the direct beam. Production fields depend on accepted climate, LSHY-2, ANUT-6 and OCN-11; any ocean integration requires explicit permission [step: baseline_run, hydrography]. A REFERENCE INTERFACE EXISTS and it is closer than expected: `vendor/cgenie/genie-plasim` is a complete Planet Simulator sharing TWENTY-FOUR OF ITS TWENTY-FIVE MODULES with `vendor/exoplasim`, the sole exception being `geniemod.f90`, the coupling itself. So a PlaSim-to-GOLDSTEIN exchange already exists against the same `pumamod` state this model carries. It moves net solar, evaporation, precipitation, runoff and two staggered wind-stress pairs to the ocean, and returns sea surface temperature, ice temperature, height, fraction and albedo, alongside area, mask, an atmospheric pressure term and CO2. Read it before specifying from first principles -- and note the term it will not give you: every exchange array is dimensioned 360, an Earth year of daily fields compiled in rather than configured, against this world's 180.7 days. What replaces that is this contract's to state. `notes/external-model-survey.md` sections 10b and 10c. AN ARCHITECTURAL FORK THIS ROW DECIDES BY IMPLICATION AND SHOULD DECIDE EXPLICITLY, 2026-08-22. The published ExoPlaSim-to-cGENIE path does NOT do what this contract specifies. It keeps EMBM running and supplies ExoPlaSim's DYNAMICS to it -- `ea_` parameters appear 13,688 times across shipped configs against 7,314 `go_`, the regrid README sets `ea_11`, and EMBM's namelist reads `xu_wstress`, `yu_wstress`, `xv_wstress`, `yv_wstress`, `u_wspeed` and `v_wspeed` from files, which is exactly what that path converts. EMBM then computes the surface heat fluxes itself, with its transport tunable through `diffamp`, `diffwid`, `difflin`, `betaz` and `betam`. THIS ROW SPECIFIES THE OTHER ARCHITECTURE: a contract carrying net and penetrative shortwave, longwave, sensible and latent heat, i.e. ExoPlaSim computing the fluxes and no EMBM. Both are viable and they cost different things. Keeping EMBM avoids OCN-5's loop but means running a second atmosphere whose transport coefficients are calibrated for Earth, on a planet with 30-hour rotation and 32 degree obliquity -- and re-tuning them so a coupled answer comes out is what failure-modes class 16 forbids. Dropping EMBM keeps ExoPlaSim as the only atmosphere and requires the loop. The second is more consistent with everything else here, but this row should CHOOSE rather than imply. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 28c. A PUBLISHED INSTANCE OF THIS CONTRACT EXISTS AND CAN BE READ OFF, 2026-08-22. cGENIE ships a PlaSim, and `vendor/cgenie/genie-plasim/src/fortran/` is a strict SUBSET of ExoPlaSim's module set plus exactly one file: `geniemod.f90`, 71 lines, pure declaration. THE WHOLE COUPLING SURFACE IS ONE ARRAY-DECLARATION MODULE, which is far smaller than this row assumes. Ocean to atmosphere, 6 used: sst, ice temperature, sea-ice height, sea-ice fraction, SEA-ICE ALBEDO, and CO2. Atmosphere to ocean, 17, each a full seasonal cycle: latent and sensible transfer COEFFICIENTS rather than fluxes, netsolar, solfor, insolar, inlong, sat, spec_hum, pressure, evap, precip, runoff, wind stress as two level pairs, and windspeed. A GENERATOR ROUTE EXISTS AND NEEDS NO GCM. muffingen is at `references/muffingen/`, GPL-3.0, and `EXAMPLE_BLANK.m` sets `par_gcm=''` with every netCDF input name empty, so it will build `.k1`, `.paths` and `.psiles` from a supplied mask and topography alone rather than from another model's output -- which is this row's route from the Orogen bathymetry. `EXAMPLE_K2_supercontinent.m` is the idealised-world precedent beside it. MUFFINGEN GENERATES SEVEN THINGS AND THIS PROJECT ALREADY HAS BETTER VERSIONS OF SEVERAL, so this contract must state which are taken and which are overridden -- the defaults are all ON. `EXAMPLE_BLANK.m` enables `opt_makemask`, `opt_maketopo`, `opt_makeocean`, `opt_makerunoff`, `opt_makewind`, `opt_makealbedo` and a sediment option. Runoff routing is `hydrography`'s, with drainage networks, catchments and a lake solver; albedo is `build_surface_albedo.py`'s and PHYS-14's; mask and bathymetry come from Orogen rather than being regridded from another model's output. WHAT ONLY MUFFINGEN KNOWS HOW TO BUILD is the grid topology, `.k1`, `.paths` and `.psiles`, and that is what to take. `opt_makezonalwind` must be OFF: the wind it would generate is a synthetic zonal profile fitted to Earth's circulation, and this world's stress is what ExoPlaSim computes at 30-hour rotation and 32 degree obliquity. `notes/external-model-survey.md` sections 18a and 18b. THE MECHANISM FOR THE FAR SIDE OF DISCHARGE EXISTS AND IS SMALL, 2026-08-22. `references/climber-x/src/geo/coast_cells.f90:115-186` delivers runoff, calving and weathering flux to N ocean cells per coastal cell, about 190 lines, with an ORDERED neighbour list: the cell itself, then 3x3 neighbours SORTED BY FEWEST OCEAN NEIGHBOURS FIRST, then a fixed 28-entry distant stencil reaching four cells in longitude. The embayment-first ordering is the non-obvious part -- freshwater goes preferentially into enclosed water rather than straight to open ocean. `river_discharge()` here stops at the land margin, so this is the liftable shape for the far side. NOTE ALSO `fix_runoff.f90`, which exists purely because the hi-res routing destination and the coarse ocean fraction disagree; that is the mesh-versus-grid disagreement this project has not hit only because discharge stops early. AND A CHOICE THIS PROJECT MADE SILENTLY: the peer's land/ocean fraction threshold is LATITUDE-DEPENDENT, `f_crit + max(0, f_crit_eq-f_crit)*cos(lat)**30`, doubled at the poles, against this project's scalar `geography_land_threshold: 0.5`. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 47e-f |

AMENDED 2026-08-22: `ClimaParams.jl` carries a block headed *Planetary and Orbital Parameters (Earth Defaults)* -- radius, gravitational acceleration, rotation rate, orbital semi-major axis, total solar irradiance, obliquity, eccentricity and longitude of perihelion -- which is an externally maintained enumeration of the quantities this audit inventories. Read it before auditing a source by hand. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 5. INVENTORY STARTED 2026-08-22 on the circulation side, and GOLDSTEIN's NON-DIMENSIONALISATION is the first hit. `initialise_goldstein.F:376-391` hardcodes `rsc = 6.37e6` and `gsc = 9.81`, Earth's radius and gravity, exposing neither, while `sodaylen`, `sidaylen`, `yearlen`, `nyear` and `par_dsc` are all in `ini_gold_nml`. Four derived scales carry the hardcoded pair into everything: `tsc = rsc/usc` sets every non-dimensional time, `rhosc = rh0sc*fsc*usc*rsc/gsc/dsc` carries both, `opsisc` scales the overturning streamfunction and `rfluxsc` the heat flux. At 1.20 Earth radii and 1.306 Earth gravity, three of those are wrong by 1.20 and `rhosc` by about eight percent, IN A MODEL THAT IS ENTIRELY NON-DIMENSIONAL -- so a reported overturning would be twenty percent low and would look plausible. AND ROTATION FAILS OPEN: `fsc` is `4*pi/sidaylen` only when solar and sidereal day lengths differ by more than 0.001, otherwise it reverts to Earth's `2*7.2921e-5` for backwards compatibility, so a configuration that sets them equal gets Earth's Coriolis scaling silently. That is the pattern this project's conventions forbid, in the one planetary constant the source does parameterise. Line 642 also records a scale having been wrong before and corrected offline. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 10e. INVENTORY EXTENDED TO THE GEOCHEMISTRY SIDE 2026-08-22, and it splits differently from the circulation side. THE CALENDAR IS COMPILE-TIME AND STATED MORE THAN ONCE: `genie-main/src/fortran/cmngem/gem_cmn.f90:585-587` declares `conv_yr_d = 365.25` as a `PARAMETER` with TWO COMMENTED ALTERNATIVES ON THE SAME LINE, 360.00 and 365.0, then `conv_yr_hr = 24.0 * conv_yr_d` with the 24-HOUR DAY AS A LITERAL, then `conv_yr_s`. Every per-year to per-second conversion in BIOGEM and SEDGEM runs through it and none of it is namelist-reachable, so the year length is a recompile. The 360.00 comment is live: the PlaSim coupling's seasonal arrays are dimensioned 360, so the codebase holds a 365.25-day geochemistry calendar beside a 360-day atmosphere coupling. `conv_yr_s` is 31,557,600 s against this world's 15,794,006, so every rate carried through it is 2.00x out. A SECOND AND INCONSISTENT STATEMENT: `sedgem_box_archer1991_sedflx.f90` hardcodes `3.15e7` TWELVE TIMES as its own seconds-per-year, converting the organic matter decay constant to a per-year rate. That is 0.183 PERCENT SHORTER than the model's own `conv_yr_s`, so a port that fixes `conv_yr_d` leaves sediment diagenesis on an Earth year and nothing fails. `gem_cmn.f90:802` likewise carries `const_rEarth = 6.37E+06` as a `PARAMETER`, a second independent statement of the radius GOLDSTEIN's `rsc` also hardcodes. THE GOOD NEWS, AND IT REDIRECTS THIS AUDIT'S EFFORT: BIOGEM's stoichiometry is NOT hardcoded -- `biogem_lib.f90:179-184` puts all four Redfield ratios in `ini_biogem_nml` and `par_bio_red_PC_flex` switches C:P to flexible entirely, so this row's "Redfield or fixed stoichiometry" item is already exposed and the fixed ratios are a default rather than an assumption. THE PORTING DIFFICULTY IS NOT UNIFORM: biogeochemical parameters are namelist-exposed, physical scaling constants are compile-time `PARAMETER`s, so an audit sampling evenly spends its effort in the wrong place. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 40. RATE TIME BASE SPLITS THREE WAYS and only one is a unit conversion, 2026-08-22 -- and note that a day inside a UNIT is invisible to a search for constants. (1) PHYSICAL/KINETIC, decided: `par_bio_remin_sinkingrate` and three siblings in `m d-1`, plus `par_bio_remin_CH4rate`, `par_scav_Fe_Ks` and `par_bio_remin_opal_K` in `d-1`, are settling speeds and reaction rates where the day is 86400 s and nothing else, so a port to a 30-hour rotation must NOT rescale them. (2) BIOLOGICAL, AMBIGUOUS: `par_bio_tau`, "biological production time-scale (days) (OCMIP-2)", was calibrated where the light cycle is 24 hours while the model resolves no diurnal cycle, so whether it moves with rotation is a modelling decision and should be recorded as an open choice rather than silently converted. (3) NOT TEMPORAL AT ALL: `par_bio_c0_I`, half saturation for light in W m-2 after Doney et al. (2006), is the marine twin of BIO-25's `CQ`; and `par_bio_I_eL` is a SINGLE SCALAR light e-folding depth in metres, which is the sharper case because its error is one-signed -- water is transparent in the blue-green and strongly absorbing in the red and near-infrared, a K dwarf puts more flux where water absorbs more, so THE PHOTIC ZONE IS SHALLOWER than a solar-calibrated e-folding depth implies. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 40d. A THIRD TIME CONVENTION, 2026-08-22, and this one carries a misleading comment. `references/climber-x/src/main/timer.f90:44-55` fixes `nday_year = 360` and `sec_year = 31556926`, the Julian year, then defines `sec_day = sec_year/day_year` with the inline comment "8.765813d4, actual seconds per day". IT IS 87658 s AND IT IS NOT A DAY -- it is a Julian year cut into 360 parts, 1.5 percent long. So every per-day rate in that model is per 1/360 Julian year. Three trees, three conventions, none configurable: cGENIE's compile-time 365.25, its separate hardcoded 3.15e7, and this. The audit should expect the convention to differ BETWEEN modules of one model, not only between models. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 42e. INVENTORY EXTENDED AGAIN 2026-08-22, and the count of independent year statements in cGENIE is SIX, not two. Beyond `conv_yr_d` and the sediment module's `3.15e7`: `genie_control.f90:190` declares `global_daysperyear = 365.25` SEPARATELY, in another module with no cross-reference, and it is what reaches the insolation routine -- so the orbital year and the biogeochemical year travel by different constants and nothing checks they agree; `initialise_genie.F:110` sets a one-hour master timestep beside an Earth solar constant; config files carry `ma_genie_timestep` as a literal second-count with a 360-day alternative commented adjacent; a dead 360-day calendar sits in `genie-lib`; AND `genie-main/runmuffin.sh:229` computes `3600.0*24.0*365.25/...` AS BASH LITERALS and appends the result to the generated config, WHICH MAKES A SHELL SCRIPT THE AUTHORITATIVE STATEMENT OF THE YEAR LENGTH for every standard run. The atmosphere's mole inventory is likewise Earth's by construction and stated three times -- `par_atm_th = 7777.0` m tuned so 1 ppm CO2 equals OCMIP's 2.123 PgC, `conv_atm_mol`, and that same literal repeated inline where the named constant was already in scope. FROM CLIMBER-X, three more of the same class: `ocn/eos.f90:85` computes ocean pressure as `p = 0.1*(-z)` bar, which is Earth gravity times seawater density WITH NO 9.81 AND NO DENSITY ON THE LINE; `constants.f90:79` welds `frac_vu = 0.45` as the sole shortwave band weight with no namelist path and no stated split wavelength, against this project's derived 0.382 at 0.75 um; and `atm_params.f90:240` welds `atm_mass = 5.12e18` kg with reference surface pressure DERIVED from it. Also THREE DIFFERENT CORIOLIS FLOORS in three components of one model -- `5e-5` as a literal in sea ice, `1e-5` in the atmosphere namelist, `5e-6` in the ocean's -- the first binding equatorward of about 20 degrees, so it replaces the Coriolis parameter over a fifth of the globe rather than guarding a small number. `notes/external-model-survey.md` sections 43c and 46b |

| OCN-17 | Evaluate the published ExoPlaSim-to-cGENIE forcing path before reusing it, and resolve the scaling factor it ships with. `acapirala/exoplasim_genie_regrid` v1.2 (`10.5281/zenodo.10802839`) converts ExoPlaSim wind stress, wind velocity and planetary albedo onto cGENIE's grid and carries topography converters both ways, and its README instructs the user to set the wind-stress scaling `ea_11`/`go_13` to 2.0 for ExoPlaSim v3.0.6 and 2.6 for v3.3.0, moving `bg_par_gastransfer_a` from 0.715 to 1.1 alongside. Identify what physical quantity that multiplier stands in for, whether it corrects the atmosphere, the regridding or the ocean's drag closure, and whether it is documented anywhere but the README; then either derive it, bracket it, or declare it. `vendor/exoplasim` is a fork of neither release, so neither pair of numbers transfers unchecked. EXPLORATION: the deliverable is an explanation of the knob and a verdict on reuse, not a coupled run | `notes/audits/ocean-and-marine-biosphere.md` sections 9b and 9f | open and CONDITIONAL on OCN-3 selecting cGENIE, though the reading is worth doing either way because the same question would arise for any prescribed stress field. A tuning multiplier on a physical forcing is a knob under this project's conventions, and inheriting one silently is exactly the failure `docs/src/practice/failure-modes.md` class 16 names. Both published couplings are one-way, so this row supplies OCN-10's atmosphere-to-ocean half and leaves OCN-5's return path and exit predicate untouched [step: baseline_run]. A MECHANISM AND A PLACE TO LOOK, from `vendor/cgenie/` 2026-08-22. cGENIE's ocean expects wind stress at TWO STAGGERED LOCATIONS, and says so itself: `genie-main/genie_loop_wrappers.f90` lines 34 and 36 annotate `ocean_stressx2_ocn` as stress at the u point and `ocean_stressx3_ocn` as stress at the v point, and `genie.F:342-360` accumulates both over `kocn_loop` steps and divides, so what arrives is a time mean of a C-grid-staggered stress. A regridding route supplying one field where two are wanted, or missing that averaging, would need a compensating multiplier of about the size this row is asking about -- and the scaling being applied on BOTH the atmosphere and the ocean side, `ea_11` and `go_13`, is what an interpolation mismatch looks like rather than a physical correction. Check that FIRST. It is a hypothesis with a mechanism, not the explanation this row still owes. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 10c. A SECOND PLACE TO LOOK, beside the staggering: muffingen itself writes `bg_par_gastransfer_a = 0.722` into the configurations it generates, and the published regrid path instructs moving that to 1.1 alongside the wind-stress scaling. So the gas transfer half of this row's knob has a generator DEFAULT to be compared against rather than only a README instruction. `references/muffingen/muffingen.m`, `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 10f. A SECOND MECHANISM, and the likelier of the two, 2026-08-22. muffingen GENERATES a synthetic zonal wind stress: `source/make_grid_winds_zonal.m` says so in its own header, and `par_tauopt` selects the regime -- case 1 annotated modern Northern Hemisphere or paleo Eocene, case 2 a water world, case 3 automatic from a zonal gateway in the mask. THE SAME SWITCH ALSO WRITES `bg_par_gastransfer_a`, with the calibration in the comments reaching 0.722. So wind stress and gas transfer are COUPLED IN THE GENERATOR BY DESIGN, and the regrid path's instruction to set the stress scaling to 2.0 or 2.6 AND move gas transfer from 0.715 to 1.1 is not two independent corrections but one adjustment about wind made in the two places muffingen couples. It also explains the 0.715: muffingen's own default is 0.722, so the path starts at the generator's value and raises it. ASK FIRST WHAT THE EXOPLASIM STRESS IS BEING COMPARED AGAINST -- muffingen's synthetic profile, or an observational product -- because the multiplier is a ratio and its denominator has not been identified. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 18c. THE MULTIPLIER IS `scf`, THE WIND STRESS SCALING, 2026-08-22, and this supersedes the two earlier hypotheses rather than adding a third. `initialise_embm.F:1195` uses it in the stress non-dimensionalisation as `...*rh0sc*dsc*usc*fsc/(rhoair*cd*scf)`, beside the drag coefficient. It appears in BOTH `ini_embm_nml` and `ini_gold_nml`, and `initialise_goldstein.F:2110` passes it on as `go_scf` -- which is why the shipped configurations set `ea_11` and `go_13` to the SAME value, 1.531013488769531300, and why the README moves both together. So the answer to what physical quantity the multiplier stands in for is: it does not stand in for one. It is a scaling that reconciles a supplied stress product against the drag closure the components were tuned with, and 2.0 or 2.6 is that factor for an ExoPlaSim-derived stress against a default tuned for cGENIE's own wind product. The C-grid staggering of section 10c and the gas-transfer coupling of 18c are consequences of the same quantity rather than separate mechanisms. WHAT REMAINS FOR THIS ROW is whether the factor should be DERIVED -- from the ratio of the two stress products over a common grid -- or declared, and it cannot be inherited because `vendor/exoplasim` is a fork of neither release the README names. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 28d. SCOPE CLARIFIED 2026-08-22: there are TWO published paths and this row tracks one. The offline `acapirala/exoplasim_genie_regrid` route moves wind stress, winds and albedo. cGENIE's in-tree `genie-plasim` is a different and fuller arrangement, handing over 17 fields including the latent and sensible transfer coefficients, both downward radiative streams and the moisture terms. Do not conflate them when resolving the scaling factor. Whether EMBM still runs under the in-tree arrangement is not settled by its declaration module and was not confirmed. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 30b |

Fix `nyear` by an explicit stability criterion rather than by the default 100, since `dt = sodaylen*yearlen/(nyear*tsc)` is a namelist consequence with no Courant check anywhere in the component. Then determine what muffingen's `.k1`, `.paths` and `.psiles` generation does above 36 x 36, whether the resolved island count exceeds the compile-time `GOLDSTEINMAXISLES` default of 10, and whether it runs under Octave at all. It matters because the tier's entire attraction is its price and that price is a property of 36 x 36 rather than of the model, so an adequacy verdict at the shipped grid and a cost verdict at a usable one are not the same measurement. Section 8b's straits, sills and partial coasts are what a finer grid would buy, and with the resolved tier dropped they ride ENTIRELY on this row: resolution is now the only lever on connectivity, so if the grid that represents them is unaffordable the outcome is a declared unresolved-connectivity bracket rather than quiet acceptance of whatever the shipped grid represents. Edwards and Marsh's finding that errors reduce only moderately with a doubling of resolution is the reason to measure that benefit rather than assume it. This row sets the ocean's own support; OCN-11 still owns the map from the 10M export, and SPAT-8 replays loop A at whatever support is chosen [step: orogen, hydrography]. PARTLY ANSWERED 2026-08-22 from the vendored tree, and this row's island figure is CORRECTED: `genie-main/genie_control.f90:79` sets `#define GOLDSTEINMAXISLES 5`, not 10. Counting the maximum island index in all 294 shipped `.psiles` files gives a range of 1 to 8, with 89 of them -- about thirty percent -- above the default. So raising the macro is ROUTINE rather than exceptional, island count is a per-geography property driving a compile-time bound, and a new geography can need a rebuild before it runs at all. That bears on OCN-11 as much as here, since this world's island count is not knowable until a candidate configuration is generated. RESOLUTION: muffingen's settings annotate `par_max_i` and `par_max_j` as `[1-72]`, but of the shipped configurations 131 are 36 x 36, five are 18 and one is 12, and nothing is above 36 -- a declared range, not a demonstrated one, which is the distinction this row exists to draw. Shipped depth is `par_max_k=17` with one extra level, and `par_A_frac_threshold=0.50` matches this project's `geography_land_threshold` in convention. OCTAVE REMAINS OPEN with the tree now local at `references/muffingen/`: it is MATLAB `.m` with a LaTeX manual under `DOCS/`, and a search finds no Octave statement either way. The manual is where to look. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 10f |
| OCN-19 | Profile the EMIC ocean at the shipped grid BEFORE optimising either half of it, then parallelise the half that pays. There is no disabled parallelism to re-enable: the OpenMP in `tstepo.F:74-90` wraps duplicate-copy experiments and the block in `biogem.f90:718-735` calls `sub_wasteCPUcycles1`/`2`, so threading is new work. Attribute runtime between the barotropic solve, tracer advection and BIOGEM's tracer loops at 36 x 36 x 16; then decompose what threads, which is BIOGEM's `do n=1,n_vocn` sweep over vectorised columns and the 3D loops in `tstepo_flux`, and NOT `ubarsolv`, whose forward elimination and back substitution both carry loop-carried dependences. Delete the dead duplication at `tstepo.F:32-35` and `62-68` first: six arrays filled every ocean timestep and read by nothing. Validate against `genie-knowngood/`, which ships per-component reference output for four configurations | `notes/audits/ocean-and-marine-biosphere.md` sections 9g and 9h | open and CONDITIONAL on OCN-3 selecting cGENIE. The profile is the deliverable that orders everything after it, and it is cheap next to either piece of work: the barotropic solve dominates GROWTH with resolution while BIOGEM's tracers may dominate RUNTIME at the shipped grid, and those are different claims that this project has not separated. The dead-copy deletion is correctness-neutral and available immediately. Threading is what makes a finer grid affordable at all, since the component currently cannot spend a second core [step: any]. CITATIONS VERIFIED 2026-08-22 against the vendored tree, and two are stronger than written. `tstepo.F` declares the six arrays at lines 32 to 35 -- two full four-dimensional tracer arrays, two more tracer copies and two density copies -- and fills all six at lines 62 to 68 every ocean timestep, while the `tstepo_flux_t` calls that would consume them are COMMENTED OUT along with the whole OpenMP block, leaving `call tstepo_flux()` as the only live call. And `sub_wasteCPUcycles1` and `2` are real subroutines at `biogem_lib.f90:2267` and `:2289`, with the block that would call them commented out too: it was a scheduling experiment on deliberately wasted cycles, so there is not merely no parallelism to re-enable, there is none to read for intent either. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 10a. THE VALIDATION SURFACE IS THINNER THAN THIS ROW IMPLIES, and it imposes an ORDER. `genie-knowngood/` is four netCDF files covering three components, not four: BIOGEM 3-D fields at 1.9 MB, an ENTS year-20 average, and GOLDSTEIN year-20 and year-10 averages, since two configurations cover the same component. Twenty and ten model years, so a short-run regression surface rather than an equilibrated comparison -- adequate for this row, worth knowing before it is asked to carry more. THE COMPARATOR IS BETTER THAN EXPECTED and already exists: `genie-main/src/c/compare.cpp` built as `nccompare.exe`, taking a relative tolerance IN ULPS and an absolute floor, which is the right measure for a reordering because it asks how far a result moved in representable numbers. THE ORDER: the GOLDSTEIN reference carries `opsi`, reported through `opsisc = dsc*usc*rsc*1e-6`, and `rsc` is Earth's radius hardcoded. So every number in the suite is a number at Earth's parameters, and the radius work under OCN-3 and OCN-12 destroys its applicability as its first act. This row is a correctness-preserving change at fixed physics, which is exactly what the suite is for -- so DO IT BEFORE the planet parameters move, or lose the validation surface. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 13. THE TWO THREADING TARGETS ARE NOT COMPARABLE, established by static analysis 2026-08-22, and this row treats them as one decomposition. BIOGEM'S SWEEP THREADS: every argument in the `do n=1,n_vocn` body is indexed by `n`, `loc_dtyr` is a read-only scalar, the reduction onto the grid happens after the loop, and both callees clear the three checks that would break it -- no `SAVE` or `DATA`, no writes to module-scope arrays since `bio_remin`, `bio_part`, `ocn` and `phys_ocn` are all reached through dummy arguments, and NO INITIALISED LOCALS anywhere in `biogem_box.f90`. That last is CLIM-51's implicit-SAVE class, which found 103 sites in `plasim/src` with no compiler flag that reveals them; across all of BIOGEM and ECOGEM the pattern appears 23 times and none is in the file holding this work. `tstepo_flux` DOES NOT: it includes `ocean.cmn` twice and that declares 44 COMMON BLOCKS, so GOLDSTEIN's state is global by construction and every variable the loop touches must be proved read-only or made private. In BIOGEM privacy is the default and sharing explicit; in GOLDSTEIN the reverse. THIS CHANGES WHAT THE PROFILE IS FOR: if it says `tstepo_flux` dominates, the answer is not "thread it" but that the threading route is expensive there and OCN-20's algorithmic route matters more, so the profile decides between two DIFFERENT KINDS of work rather than two candidates for the same kind. Caveats on the counts: they are an upper bound that does not separate module-scope declarations from procedure-body ones, and this is one level into the call tree. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 14 |
| OCN-20 | Replace the barotropic streamfunction solve with one that does not grow as r^3 per timestep, and prove it against the existing reference output. `invert.f:32` orders the Psi points lexicographically as `k = i + j*n`, which is what fixes the bandwidth at `imax` and makes `ubarsolv`'s two triangular sweeps cost about `2*imax*(jmax+1)*(imax+1)` per ocean timestep with `gap(mpxi*mpxj, 2*mpxi+3)` in storage. Price a fill-reducing reordering against an iterative treatment of the elliptic streamfunction equation, and cost the island machinery FIRST, since `ratm`, `psisl`, `erisl` and `matinv_gold` all assume the present factorisation and `GOLDSTEINMAXISLES` bounds them. Any iterative scheme changes results to within a declared tolerance rather than exactly, so fix the tolerance and the acceptance comparison before choosing | `notes/audits/ocean-and-marine-biosphere.md` sections 9g and 9h | open and CONDITIONAL on OCN-3 selecting cGENIE and on OCN-19's profile showing this half is worth the work at a grid this project would actually run. It is the algorithmic complement to OCN-19 rather than an alternative: threads cannot help a sequential recurrence, and a reordering cannot help the tracer loops. Thresholds are fixed before results are seen, so the tolerance against `genie-knowngood/` is declared in the same commit as the implementation and not after the first comparison [step: any]. A CORRECTNESS ARGUMENT BESIDE THE COMPLEXITY ONE, found on verifying this row's citation. `invert.f:32` carries `k=i + j*n` exactly as quoted, and three lines above it sits an acknowledged and unresolved out-of-bounds: a comment saying gfortran will flag an array reference out of bounds, that it is all OK, and then "(I think ...)". So the routine this row proposes to replace has a defect its own authors recorded and did not settle. This project can settle it cheaply -- `-ffpe-trap` is in the production flag line and the checked profile carries `-fcheck=all`, which caught a real out-of-bounds during the CLIM-40 fold work -- and doing so before any replacement is designed says whether the reordering is a performance change or a repair. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 10a. THE TOLERANCE INSTRUMENT EXISTS, so this row's requirement to fix it before choosing needs no new tooling: `genie-main/src/c/compare.cpp`, built as `nccompare.exe`, takes `-r` as a relative tolerance IN ULPS and `-a` as an absolute floor. Units in the last place is the right measure for an iterative scheme replacing a direct one, because it asks how far the answer moved in representable numbers rather than as a fraction. AND THE SAME ORDERING APPLIES AS OCN-19's: the reference fields are overturning streamfunctions scaled by `opsisc`, which carries Earth's hardcoded `rsc`, so this replacement must be validated BEFORE the radius work moves the parameters, or its acceptance comparison stops existing. `notes/external-model-survey.md` sections 13b and 13c |
| OCN-21 | The sea ice is thermodynamic-only and that is an undeclared structural term rather than a decision. `icemod.f90` runs thermodynamics with no ice dynamics and no transport, which is the same configuration BIG-MITgcm runs and names as a stated limitation, reporting excessive ice as its consequence at a comparable resolution. So the term is not negligible on peer evidence, and nothing here has priced it or written it down. `references/climber-x/src/sic` is the reference implementation in this model class, `sic_dyn.f90` and `transport_sic.f90` | `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 3f, `notes/audits/absent-and-inherited-physics.md` | open and BLOCKED, and the blocker is physical rather than a queue: sea ice dynamics is driven by wind stress and ocean currents, and this ocean is a slab with horizontal transport deliberately off under CLIM-16, so a dynamics scheme would be advecting ice across a motionless ocean. What is NOT blocked and is what this row asks for first: state the term in `analysis/error_budget.json` as a structural item beside no-q-flux, with the direction taken from the peer report rather than guessed, so the omission is declared where a reader of the budget will find it. The scheme itself follows the ocean and not this row [step: baseline_run]. SHAPE CORRECTED 2026-08-22, and the block confirmed. This row says sea ice dynamics is driven by wind stress and ocean currents; the EMIC-class answer needs only the second. `vendor/cgenie/genie-goldsteinseaice/tstepsic.f` announces itself as a "2nd order explicit transport code using upper level ocean velocities", and `tstipsic.f` builds its advective coefficients from `u(1,i,j)` and `u(2,i,j)` with `diffsic` beside them. There is NO ice momentum equation, no internal stress, no wind stress on the floe, and a separate `uice` field exists commented out at every use site. So the ladder runs: thermodynamic only here, advection by upper-ocean velocity in cGENIE, and a full dynamic-thermodynamic scheme in CLIMBER-X's `sic_dyn.f90`. THE BLOCK STANDS EXACTLY -- a slab ocean has no velocity field, so there is nothing to advect with -- BUT THE EVENTUAL FIX IS SMALLER than this row implies: an advection term on two prognostic fields using a velocity the ocean would already produce. That strengthens the case for declaring the term in the error budget NOW, since the declaration is not deferring an expensive decision. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 19. RELATED, 2026-08-22: in cGENIE's in-tree PlaSim coupling the sea-ice ALBEDO is supplied by the ocean model as `genie_alb_sic`, so PlaSim's own ice albedo pair does not set it. That is the pair which survives `radmod`'s open-ocean overwrite and which PHYS-14 owns, so a future coupling moves it out of this project's hands rather than needing it fixed first. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 30b. PRICED, AND THE BLOCK IS SCHEME-DEPENDENT RATHER THAN ABSOLUTE, 2026-08-22 -- verified in source before acting on it. THE COST, from CLIMBER-X's EVP scheme (Hunke and Dukowicz 1997, elliptical yield curve, C-grid, from SIS2): FIVE new prognostic 2-D fields in the restart, `ui`, `vi` and the three stress-tensor components; ice concentration PROMOTED to prognostic and separately advected, a sixth; five forcing fields in; an EVP subcycle of 50 steps per sea-ice step against an SIS2 default of 432; one flux-corrected transport pass per advected field; and guards it cannot run without -- a velocity clamp at `cfl_fac*dx/dt` with `cfl_fac = 0.1` "for CFL stability near the Poles", a viscosity cap, and a stress limiter called every step. THE BLOCK: this row says a slab ocean has nothing to advect with, which is exactly right for cGENIE's advection-by-upper-ocean-velocity and NOT right for EVP, which is a MOMENTUM solve. `sic_dyn.f90:603` forms the forcing as `(Cor + PFu)*mi_u + (fxic_now + tauxa)` -- Coriolis, sea-surface tilt, internal ice stress and WIND STRESS -- with ocean velocity entering only as the drag reference at `:593`. With `uo = vo = ssh = 0` the tilt vanishes and drag becomes drag against still water, while wind stress, internal stress and Coriolis remain. Wind-driven ice over a motionless ocean is a reduced but coherent configuration, which is what free-drift approximations do. THE ROW'S CONCLUSION IS UNCHANGED -- declare the term in the error budget first -- but restate the block as applying to one family of schemes rather than to sea-ice motion. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 43a-b |

| PHYS-13 | Orogen places the ice that carves this terrain by latitude and dimensionless elevation alone. At the build's `glacialErosion` of 0.8 the ice line falls at 58 degrees latitude, positioned by the erosion slider and nothing else, and the altitude gate is in the model's elevation parameter so the snowline tracks maximum relief, which scales as 1/g, rather than a freezing altitude set by lapse rate and surface temperature. `glacIdx` consults no temperature at all: not the K2.5V spectrum, not the 32 degree obliquity, not the 30 hour rotation, and not even Orogen's own temperature field, which `computeTemperature` produces after `runPostProcessing` has already eroded. All three leading parameters point the same way, toward less polar ice than an Earth-tuned 58 degree threshold gives | `notes/audits/orogen-resolution.md` | open. The correction needs no new physics, but NOT the field first proposed here. `glac` is identically zero across all twelve bins and all 8,192 cells while the run manifest records `"glaciers": true`, and `snd` peaks at 0.29 m, which is seasonal snow. That zero is a fact about the grid, not the planet: the sub-grid peak excess `orog_max - orog_mean` on land averages 1.065 km and 2.969 km at the 90th percentile, so at the measured warm-season lapse rate of 7.372 K/km the model evaluates its own high ground about 7.8 K too warm on average and 21.9 K too warm in the top tenth, and no ice survives that. USE INSTEAD the warmest-month surface temperature, which is smooth and genuinely resolved at T42, corrected to each mesh region's own elevation through `lib/lapse.py`'s freezing-height criterion. `maps/build_basemap.py` lines 379-385 already implement exactly this for the basemap's ice shading. Measured that way, land below freezing in the warmest month goes from 0.002% on the model's orography to 1.657% on the 15.19 km mesh, and `analysis/ice_mask_freezing_height.py` is the registered computation. Raising the model truncation is not the alternative: T85 is about 150 km against a 7.59 km mesh, so the peak excess stays sub-grid at any resolution the model can afford. Drive `glacIdx` from that mask instead of `polarDist` and close it through loop A, which exists to feed a commissioned climate into the next generation; the terrain-before-climate ordering that blocks it inside one pass is not a constraint across iterations. SHAPE OF THE FIX, decided 2026-08-20: an ice mask consumed AT GENERATION like the carve list, `--ice-mask FILE`, not a post-hoc terrain alteration. Glacial, hydraulic and thermal erosion share one iteration loop and a mid-loop priority flood at `GLACIAL_MID_FLOOD_FRAC` exists to cut outlets through the depressions glaciation makes, so a separate later pass would leave them undrained and move the drainage network and the basin catalogue. Bootstrap with `--glacial 0`, an honest null, then commission and regenerate with the mask. Two constraints on it: mapping `glac` from the ExoPlaSim grid back to the mesh is a rule 3 crossing and must go by index or a shared coordinate source, and per `docs/src/reference/no-time-axis.md` a current ice field can only say WHERE ice was, never how long, so the strength slider stays a declared choice rather than a measurement. Subsumes the mechanism half of GRAV-6 [step: orogen]. AMENDED 2026-08-22: this row is also upstream of any ice sheet mass balance, not only of the carve terrain. Every candidate scheme in this class drives ablation from surface temperature, which makes the 7.8 K mean and 21.9 K top-tenth warm bias measured here the single most damaging input error such a scheme can be given. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 4 |
| PHYS-14 | Snow, glacier ice and sea ice albedo are spectrally FLAT and solar-weighted, and they are the only surface endmembers never re-derived for this star. `plasimmod.f90:428-432` declares `dsnowalbmx(2) = 0.8`, `dsnowalbmn(2) = 0.4` and `dglacalbmn(2) = 0.6` -- two-element BAND arrays initialised from a scalar, so the simulation's snow reflects identically either side of the 0.75 um split. That is the same defect `config/planet.yaml` records having fixed for vegetation, where "one identical field used to assert that a canopy reflects equally either side of the split". It also means the k25v spectrum fix did nothing for snow: the two-band machinery carries a star's shape by moving flux BETWEEN bands, and moving flux between two equal numbers changes nothing. THE DERIVATION IS DONE AND THE NUMBERS ARE IN THE NOTE. `exoplasim/surfacespecs.py` ships the ECOSTRESS blends the constants came from, `iceblendmax`, `iceblendmin`, `glacalbmin`, `seaicemax` and `seaicemin`, interpolated to the same 965 wavelengths `k25v.dat` uses, and only `pRT.py` reads them. Weighted by a 5772 K Planck they reproduce 0.8, 0.4 and 0.6 to within 0.016, WHICH IS THE CHECK THAT VALIDATES THE INTEGRAL before anything is concluded from the other column. SIX FOR SIX on that check, the worst by 0.016 and the ocean to three decimals. Weighted by `k25v` the band pairs are 0.983/0.585, 0.508/0.273, 0.764/0.406, 0.892/0.473 and 0.636/0.339, with broadbands 0.736, 0.362, 0.541, 0.631 and 0.451 -- so fresh snow is about 0.064 too bright, old snow 0.038, glacier ice 0.059, MAXIMUM SEA ICE 0.069 and minimum sea ice 0.049, every one in the direction that weakens the ice-albedo feedback at the cold end. Sea ice is the largest single error in the set and the one surface with no configuration key at all, so `dicealbmx` and `dicealbmn` are in this row's scope beside the land three. The OCEAN is exempt and that is a result rather than an omission: `oceanblend` moves 0.070 to 0.068 because water is nearly flat across the split, so it does not earn a key | `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 11, `notes/audits/inherited-earth-constants.md`, `docs/src/practice/failure-modes.md` class 31 | open, and what is left is SHAPE rather than derivation: a provenance-stamped analysis product on `analysis/vegetation_albedo.py`'s pattern, an anchoring convention for the band pair like `vegetation_albedo_bands` so the flux-weighted combination reproduces the broadband value, keys in `config/planet.yaml`, and a decision on the sea ice pair, which belongs to `icemod` rather than `landmod` and has no config key at all today. `iceblendmax` against `iceblendmin` is already the grain and impurity bracket this row asked for, so that is answered too. HIGH LEVERAGE and unchanged: these drive the feedback that dominates the cold end, so this reaches the bare-rock endmember arm, PHYS-13's ice mask, the extreme-cold land fraction capping the design flux, and what the stellar cycle's grand minima are worth [step: surface_albedo, bootstrap_run]. THE SHAPE QUESTION IS SETTLED BY PRECEDENT ON BOTH SIDES, 2026-08-22. ExoCAM's modified CLM sets `albice(numrad) = (/ 0.80, 0.55 /)`, land ice albedo by waveband, under a header saying the CLM defaults are too high and citing Paterson (1994) p.59 for a FULL-SPECTRAL value of about 0.5 with CAM3 and Pritchard et al. (2008) as precedent. Two things follow. The two bands hold DIFFERENT numbers in an independent implementation, which confirms the flat arrays here are anomalous rather than conventional. And the values are anchored against a stated full-spectral target, which is the same convention `vegetation_albedo_bands` already uses -- so the anchoring rule this row needed is settled: the band pair reproduces the broadband value, and the broadband value is the one derived from the spectrum. As a sanity check the numbers line up the right way: section 11's `glacalbmin` gives 0.766/0.455 under the Sun against CLM's 0.80/0.55, lower because `glacalbmin` is the GLACIAL MINIMUM blend, old or dirty ice, while Paterson's 0.5 is a general glacier figure. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 22. A PREDICTOR THIS MODEL DOES NOT HAVE FOR SNOW, AND IT IS WORTH AS MUCH AS THE ONE IT DOES, 2026-08-22, and ORTHOGONAL to the spectral reweighting this row already settled. ExoPlaSim's snow albedo is a linear ramp in SURFACE TEMPERATURE between 263.16 K and `tmelt`, `landmod.f90:394-405`, blended by forest fraction, with no zenith term. ClimaLand's is `min(1 - beta*(rho_snow/rho_liq - x0), 1) * (alpha_0 + d_alpha*exp(-k*cos_z))` -- zenith angle and snow density as a grain-size proxy, with no temperature term. Disjoint predictors for the same physics. At ClimaLand's calibrated `alpha_0 = 0.59`, `d_alpha = 0.40`, `k = 1.96` and the density factor 0.874 at 330 kg/m3, the ZENITH TERM SPANS 0.565 TO 0.865, a range of 0.30, against this model's ENTIRE temperature range of 0.40 from `dsnowalbmn` to `dsnowalbmx`. Flux-weighted daily means at 32 degrees obliquity run 0.599 at the equator equinox to 0.783 at 60 degrees winter, an 0.18 swing that is absent, largest in the winter hemisphere at high latitude where the snow is. NOTE THE INTERNAL INCONSISTENCY IT EXPOSES: section 29 established that ExoPlaSim applies a zenith-dependent albedo to open ocean BY DEFAULT, so the same physical effect is present for one surface and absent for another inside one model, and both cases live in the same low-sun regime. ClimaLand's values are Earth solar-spectrum calibrations and do not transfer; the form and the magnitude argument do. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 37b-c. THE MISSING PREDICTOR IMPLEMENTED, PLUS A DUST PATH, 2026-08-22. `references/climber-x/src/sic/surface_par_sic.f90:111-260` carries two snow-albedo schemes -- Warren-Wiscombe and Dang et al. (2015) -- BOTH taking prognostic grain size AND dust concentration, with grain size corrected for solar zenith angle. Snow grain size is a prognostic in the restart. That is section 37's missing zenith predictor implemented in an EMIC, with a second predictor beside it this project has no representation of at all. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 43f |
| PHYS-15 | Soil albedo carries no moisture dependence, and the blocker is a missing state variable rather than a missing parameterisation. `dalbclim1` and `dalbclim2` at `landmod.f90:118-119` are PRESCRIBED STATIC fields on the same 0.75 um band split this project uses, and this project's own albedo products are moisture-blind too -- `analysis/rock_albedo.py`, `analysis/playa_albedo.py` and the surface-class builder all lack a wet/dry axis. Wet ground is darker than dry ground, so on land with a strong seasonal wetting cycle this is a seasonal albedo term with no representation. The parameterisation is standard: ClimaLand's `CLMTwoBandSoilAlbedo` after Lawrence and Chase (2007) is `alpha_band = alpha_dry*(1-S_e) + alpha_wet*S_e` on exactly that band pair, so it drops into the existing structure. WHAT IS ABSENT IS `S_e`: it is the effective saturation over the top 0.02 m, and ExoPlaSim's land is one scalar bucket with no depth, so there is nothing to evaluate it from | `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 39 | open and BLOCKED on LSHY-3, which is the only route to a near-surface saturation; nothing here is actionable before a land column exists, and this row is the reason to note that LSHY-3 enables a CLIMATE feedback and not only a hydrological one. WHEN UNBLOCKED the deliverable is the dry/wet endmember pair per surface class under this star, derived the way BIO-18 and PHYS-14 derive theirs, and NOT the Earth MODIS fields those references carry -- their values are spatially varying Earth calibrations and only the FORM transfers [step: vegetation_albedo, surface_albedo] IT SHARES DUST-17'S ARTIFACT AND MUST NOT DEFINE A SECOND. DUST-17 already specifies the top emitting-layer LIQUID water state this needs -- emitting depth, gravimetric conversion from LSHY-1 bulk density and texture, frozen water, capillary and groundwater wetting, inundation and wet/dry mosaic area -- and explicitly forbids "a second central hydrology". A near-surface saturation defined here would BE that second hydrology. So this row consumes DUST-17's state rather than deriving one, and the depth conversion is already stated once in `aeolian/config/dust.yaml:108-120` and reaches the model as the single namelist constant `dustwcv`. THE TWO ROWS DIFFER ONLY IN THE DEPTH THEY READ AT: Fecan's threshold correction and CLM's albedo saturation are both surface-skin quantities but need not share one number, so the emitting depth and the albedo depth are separate declarations off one profile. NOT A GAP, checked 2026-08-22: pedology takes RUNOFF rather than soil moisture for weathering and `build_soil.py:301-303` argues it -- leaching needs water to drain through the profile, and rain that falls and evaporates carries nothing -- so soil formation is a deliberate choice here and not an omission |

A UNIT TRAP THIS PORT WILL HIT, AND A REFERENCE IMPLEMENTATION, 2026-08-22. ClimaLand's `plant_hydraulics.jl` is an independent implementation of what this row ports: the plant as a Darcy medium, `F = -K_eff*((psi2-psi1)/(z2-z1) + 1)` at `:197` with a harmonic-mean conductivity, and a Weibull vulnerability curve `K = K_sat*exp(-(psi/psi63)**c)` at `:256` after Liu (2020) and Sperry (2016), both per-PFT. THE TRAP IS THE UNIT. That gravitational term is written as a bare `+1`, which is right only because `psi` is in metres of HEAD, and head is `P/(rho*g)`, so the convention absorbs gravity and hides it. `toml/default_parameters.toml:49-53` stores `psi_63 = -408.163...` whose own description reads "Computed by -4 / 0.0098. Holtzman's original parameter value is -4 MPa" -- 0.0098 MPa/m being `rho*g` at EARTH gravity. So the stored number is a plant property times Earth. The same -4 MPa here is -312.26 m of head, exactly `1/1.306` of it. AND THE USE SITE MAKES IT WORSE: `soil_moisture_stress.jl:82` converts back with `psi*rho_water*grav`, so carrying `psi_63 = -408.16` forward while setting `grav` to 12.81 commits the error TWICE and presents the stress function with -5.22 MPa where the literature says -4. THE RULE TO CARRY: any plant-hydraulics parameter published as a PRESSURE and stored as a HEAD carries the gravity of whoever stored it, and nothing in the stored value says so. Failure-mode class 32 in its unit form. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 41. AND AN INDEPENDENT MODEL PUTS THE SAME CEILING IN A MORTALITY TERM, 2026-08-22. FATES uses `grav_earth` in exactly two places and one is plant hydraulics, as the gravitational head of the plant water column. At 12.81 m/s2 that head term rises 30.7 percent, raising the fractional loss of conductivity at a given soil water potential and therefore raising hydraulic-failure mortality -- **effectively capping tree height through a mortality rate rather than an allometric limit**. That is section 41c's hydraulic ceiling arriving by a second route in a second model, which strengthens the case for treating it as physics rather than as an allometric convention. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 54d |

WET-12 names the reduced form of this classification, which is the TOPMODEL saturated fraction from GW-26's index; it is the part of the fallback that forfeits least, and mutual exclusivity is what it gives up. WHY THE LATITUDE PROHIBITION BITES, PRICED 2026-08-22. The fork's decomposition already selects by latitude and the step is large: `somdynam.cpp` gives a saturated mineral soil a moisture modifier of 0.360 on every pool, against 0.025 on the slow and passive pools for a high-latitude peatland stand, a factor of 14.4 on the long-lived carbon, switched at `PEATLAND_WETLAND_LATITUDE_LIMIT = 40.0`. NOTE ALSO that the general curve, Friend et al. (1997) Eqn 53 after Parton et al. (1993), BOTTOMS OUT AT 0.360 at full saturation rather than falling to zero -- so on that curve alone saturation does not preserve carbon, and the classification this row derives is doing all the work. THE MECHANISTIC ALTERNATIVE that needs no category: ClimaLand's DAMM implementation limits respiration by oxygen as `theta_a**(4/3)` through a Michaelis-Menten factor, so anoxia is a computed consequence of air-filled porosity rather than a stand type. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 34. THE INUNDATION SCHEME IS SMALLER THAN EXPECTED AND ITS COST IS PREPROCESSING, 2026-08-22. CaMa-Flood's ENTIRE floodplain representation is one precomputed array per cell -- a TEN-BIN CDF OF HEIGHT ABOVE NEAREST DRAINAGE, stored at each decile of flooded AREA -- turned at init into a storage-versus-level curve of ten nested prisms. The runtime prognostic state is TWO SCALARS, channel and floodplain storage; depth, flooded fraction and area are all diagnostic, recovered by walking the storage bins. The closure making it work is that channel and floodplain share one water surface elevation. THE COST IS NOT THE PHYSICS: `fldhgt` is not generated anywhere in the CaMa tree -- it comes from an external DEM upscaling package -- so adopting this means writing the HAND-CDF preprocessor against the region mesh ourselves. AND HAND DOES NOT EXIST HERE: `build_hydrography.py` computes level/area/volume curves at 128 levels but PER CLOSED BASIN from the priority-flood ordering, which is a lake curve. CaMa's is per routing cell keyed on height above the channel. Different quantity, same word. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 52a |

WET-12 names the reduced form, a constant anaerobic respiration fraction, and records that it forfeits exactly what this row exists to expose: production and oxidation cease to be separable. SUBSTRATE AND REDOX HAVE A MECHANISTIC REFERENCE FORM, 2026-08-22. ClimaLand implements DAMM, Davidson et al. (2012), in `Soil/Biogeochemistry/co2_parameterizations.jl`: respiration is `Vmax(T) * MM_sx * MM_o2` with substrate `Sx = p_sx*Csom*D_liq*theta_l**3` and oxygen availability `D_oa*O2_f*theta_a**(4/3)` on Millington-Quirk tortuosity, each entering through its own Michaelis-Menten factor. That gives limitation at BOTH ends of the moisture range from two separate mechanisms, against this fork's single empirical humped curve plus a categorical override. It is the shape this row's vertically-resolved substrate/saturation/redox consumption would take. Its Arrhenius term is written in centered form so the reference rate and the temperature sensitivity are near-orthogonal under calibration, which is worth copying. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 34a SCOPE LIMITS ON DAMM, from the paper rather than the port: it is a RESPIRATION KERNEL for soluble substrates, offered by its authors as a core module informed by other modules for microbial dynamics and substrate supply, so it replaces CENTURY's temperature and moisture response functions and NOT its pool structure; and it was evaluated on lab enzyme assays plus one trenched plot at Harvard Forest, so the FORM transfers and the coefficients are an Earth site calibration. `references/davidson2012-damm-dual-arrhenius-michaelis-menten.pdf`. A SECOND MODEL WITH THE SAME DEFECT, AND A PUBLISHED BRACKET, 2026-08-22. CLIMBER-X emits land methane as a fraction of soil respiration, and `nml/lnd_par.nml:236-238` sets `ch4_frac_wet = 0.082` and `ch4_frac_peat = 0.082` -- ONE TUNED NUMBER FOR TWO RESERVOIRS -- with its own comments recording that it sits OUTSIDE the cited published range for BOTH: above Spahni 2011's wetland 0.024-0.0415 and below Spahni's peat 0.2-0.25, against Riley 2011's 0.2 for wetlands. The published values put peat and wetland fractions five to ten times apart and this model collapses them. That raises this row's evidence for globally-tuned ratios from one model to two, and supplies a STARTING BRACKET that is published rather than invented. Its peat path does carry the oxic/anoxic split the wetland path lacks, `f_oxic_peat = w_table_peat/acro_h`. Temperature modulation is `q10_ch4 = 1.8` referenced to 295 K, another Earth anchor. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 42c |
| WET-7 | Planet-port and bracket methane transport: replace fixed gravity, 101325 Pa, atmospheric O2/CH4, zero wind, 24-hour velocity and Earth-day gas substeps with BIO-22/BIO-23/BIO-28 inputs; test diffusion, plant transport and ebullition conservation/restart continuity; and bound dead-tiller, winter/thaw, pressure/wind, nucleation and unsaturated-bubble-oxidation pathways | `biosphere/notes/wetlands-peat-methane-audit.md` finding 7 | open; no-simulation source/state tests can proceed with WET-1/WET-3, while pathway response cases depend on WET-5/WET-6 and explicit permission [step: lpj_driver, lpj_run] |
