WET-12 names PALADYN's expansion and contraction rules as the reduced form of these transition semantics, capped at 1 percent areal change a year with a minimum fraction seeding every cell |
| WET-10 | Close accepted seasonal methane fluxes, including any separately conserved OCN-13 marine source, through BVOC-6's Vesper oxidant/transport bracket: solve lifetime, OH feedback, atmospheric imbalance and global/latitudinal CH4, feed only a converged provenance-bound abundance to ExoPlaSim's radiative band, and prohibit combining modelled sources with the fixed-modern-Earth-flux 1.6 ppmv state | `biosphere/notes/wetlands-peat-methane-audit.md` finding 10, `notes/audits/ocean-and-marine-biosphere.md` section 8d | open; reduced chemistry interface follows WET-8/BVOC-6 and admits OCN-13 only as a named source, not a second atmospheric owner; replacement of `pCH4_bar` and every chemistry/climate response experiment require accepted closure and explicit permission [step: lpj_run, baseline_climatology, baseline_run]. A reduced form of the LIFETIME half exists and this row should be costed against it: `references/climber-x/src/ch4/ch4_model.f90` is a partitioned-lifetime box, tropospheric OH against separate chlorine, soil and stratospheric sinks, with an OH temperature sensitivity and OH sensitivities to CO, NOx and VOC precursors. The STRUCTURE transfers and the calibration does not: the precursor sensitivities are fitted to anthropogenic Earth emissions read from a file, the chlorine term is Earth stratospheric chemistry, and the OH field is a photochemical product of the host's ultraviolet -- which for this star is not an open question, since `atmosphere.pCH4_bar` already records that Rugheimer et al. (2013) computed the photochemical steady state at this effective temperature. A constant respiration fraction still yields a flux and never an abundance, so `atmosphere.pCH4_bar` stays this row's alone. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 3f. A ROUTE, NOT JUST A FLOOR, 2026-08-22. `vendor/cgenie/genie-atchem/atchem_box.f90` carries six methane oxidation schemes. The default is Osborn and Wigley (1994), `tau = tau0*(CH4/C0)**N`, with the comment "omitting [OH], [NOx] etc etc" and a clamp at half the reference concentration where the calibration curve ends. **`sub_calc_oxidize_CH4_schmidt03` is the same functional form with the constants EXPOSED** as `par_pCH4_oxidation_tau0`, `C0` and `N` rather than hardcoded, and its header names its calibration: a fit to the 2-D photochemistry model of Schmidt and Shindell (2003). So this row's oxidant and lifetime half reduces to DERIVING THREE NUMBERS from a photochemistry calculation for this host -- and `atmosphere.pCH4_bar` already records that Rugheimer et al. (2013) computed the photochemical steady state at this effective temperature, which is the same class of calculation. The conservation structure comes with it and is what this row wants: `O2 -= 2*fracdecay*CH4`, `CO2 += fracdecay*CH4` with 13C and 14C carried, `CH4 *= (1-fracdecay)`, per grid cell rather than as a global box. LEAVE THE `claire06` AND `goldblatt06` FAMILIES ALONE: they take a whole-atmosphere conversion factor with no grid indices and are Archean anoxic-atmosphere formulations, built for a kind of planet this one is not. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 20a. THE MASS-TO-CONCENTRATION CONVERSION IS A PLANET PROPERTY AND MUST BE DERIVED, NOT INHERITED, 2026-08-22. Any box-model methane budget needs a Tg-to-ppb factor, and CLIMBER-X's `src/ch4/ch4_model.f90:40` derives its 2.7476 Tg CH4 per ppb from EARTH's atmospheric mass of 5.1480e18 kg (IPCC, Prather et al. 2012). Column mass is `P/g` over `4*pi*R^2`, so with this world's 1 bar summed from `planet.yaml`'s partial pressures, 1.20 Earth radii and 12.81 m/s2, the atmosphere is 1.088x Earth's and the factor becomes 2.990 Tg CH4 per ppb: A GIVEN SOURCE IN Tg/yr PRODUCES ABOUT 92 PERCENT OF THE ppb IT WOULD ON EARTH. The same rescaling applies to any N2O or CO2 budget. ALSO A SPIN-UP CONSTRAINT for the seasonal budget this row closes: CLIMBER-X derives peat potential from a THIRTY-YEAR inundation memory, a 360-month ring buffer at `src/lnd/lnd_params.f90:379`, so a peat fraction cannot be established from one model year. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 42b and 42d. THE LIFETIME IS COMPUTABLE AND THE DOOR IS NAMED, 2026-08-22. CAABA/MECCA carries the sink as real chemistry -- `CH4 + OH` with a modified-Arrhenius rate, OH from `H2O + O1D`, O1D from ozone photolysis -- and carries NO prescribed lifetime anywhere, so it must be derived offline as `1/(k*[OH])`. THE BLOCKING QUESTION IS ANSWERED NEGATIVELY FOR JVAL: its fitted cross-section coefficients were generated with a hardcoded 176-element ATLAS-2 SOLAR spectrum as the integration weight, so a different star needs that array replaced and the preprocessor re-run over a 19,393-line generated file. THE TRACTABLE DOOR IS CLOUDJ: `input/cloudj/FJX_spec.txt` holds the solar photon flux as EIGHTEEN NUMBERS in a text file, and CAABA accepts `photrat_channel='cloudj'`. CAVEAT THAT MUST NOT BE DROPPED: whether CLOUDJ has ever been exercised in this release is NOT determined -- there is no `caaba_cloudj.nml` and no testsuite entry where RADJIMT and TRAJECT both have one. Build-check before planning around it. SEPARATELY, `messy_jval.f90:1544` converts pressure to overhead column as `N_A/(M_air*g)` with Earth gravity, making every O2 and O3 slant column 1.306x too large here regardless of spectrum. AND THE SCOPE LIMIT: a box model gives a LOCAL chemical destruction rate, not a global lifetime -- that needs the local rate weighted over the atmosphere's mass and OH distribution, an offline integration over an ExoPlaSim climatology using a grid of box results. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 48 |

| WET-12 | DECLARE the reduced wetland, peat and methane form and what taking each part COSTS IN CLAIMS, so a fallback is a decision with a procedure rather than a retreat taken under pressure. WET-1 through WET-11 specify a target; nothing has yet shown that chain is implementable within this project, and it rests on SDEC-3's vertical coordinate, PLHY-4 and PLHY-5, PCAR-5's trait registry and BVOC-6's oxidant bracket, all wholly open. PALADYN is a design that works, is published and runs at this project's throughput, so it is the floor each part can fall back to. THE THREE PARTS ARE NOT EQUALLY VIABLE and that is the substance. EXTENT is genuinely supplied: wetland fraction is the TOPMODEL saturated fraction where the surface is snow free, from GW-26's index, and potential peatland is the fraction wet at least three months of the year; it forfeits only WET-2's mutual exclusivity, which is bookkeeping rather than physics. PEAT STOCK is supplied at a nameable cost: acrotelm and catotelm confined to the top soil layer, transfer at a critical acrotelm carbon, catotelm shifted down as density fills a layer, peat carbon vertical diffusivity zero, areal change capped at 1 percent a year with a minimum fraction seeding every cell; it forfeits WET-4's vertical resolution and with it every depth-resolved redox claim. METHANE is a bracket and never a measurement: a constant fraction of heterotrophic respiration wherever respiration is anaerobic, one fraction per surface type, which cannot separate production from oxidation and contains no transport, so WET-6's independent exposure and WET-7's diffusion, plant and ebullition pathways collapse into the constant | `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 3e, `biosphere/notes/wetlands-peat-methane-audit.md` | open, and a DECLARATION rather than an implementation: the deliverable is the reduced form written down with its per-part cost and the condition for taking it, BEFORE anything is built to it, so the comparison is made while neither option is under schedule pressure. The condition is PER PART and not global -- a part falls back when a consumer needs the field and the dependency that part waits on does not exist -- and any fallback is labelled ON THE ARTIFACT, so nothing downstream can read a reduced field as a full one. ONE PROHIBITION SURVIVES INTACT: the methane part yields a surface FLUX under a transplanted-Earth assumption and never an abundance, so it may not move `atmosphere.pCH4_bar` on its own. The lifetime half that would convert the one into the other belongs to WET-10, and it has a reduced form of its own that WET-10 names [step: soil, groundwater, lpj_driver] |

| SPAT-11 | Establish what each rung of the resolution ladder COSTS in wall clock, because SPAT-8 defers cost to "record cost separately" and nothing in this tree says what a rung is worth. That number is the justification for the whole optimisation workstream -- SHTns, the thread port and FP32 are aimed at the ladder rather than at T42 -- so it is currently the one term in that argument nobody has written down. THE INPUTS ARE MEASURED AND THE ARITHMETIC IS NOT THE PROBLEM: 77 to 93 s per orbit at T42 L10 on 16 ranks, a Legendre work ratio of 7.9x at T85 and 26.2x at T127 against grid work of 4.0x and 9.0x, transform shares of 5.5, 10.4 and 14.9 percent across those rungs, and a measured 39.7 percent from single precision. Composed naively those give roughly 240 local years a day at T85 against T42's thousand. THE MISSING TERM IS THE TIMESTEP and it is not small: `timestep_minutes` is 45 at T42 and a finer grid needs a shorter step for stability, so a factor of two there halves every projection and the composed number moves from 240 to about 120. `exoplasim/notes/non-mpi-performance-roadmap.md` already names measuring the largest scientifically acceptable timestep at each resolution as its first priority item, which is the same measurement | `exoplasim/notes/spectral-transform-profile.md`, `exoplasim/notes/non-mpi-performance-roadmap.md`, `notes/audits/pyburn-postprocessing-cost.md` | open. EXPLORATION: the deliverable is a cost per rung with its timestep stated, and a BRACKET rather than a point wherever the timestep is assumed rather than measured, because a projection that hides an unmeasured factor of two is worse than a bracket that shows it. It needs no production run: the timestep question is answerable on a short integration per rung and the rest is arithmetic over numbers already recorded. ORDER IT BEFORE committing to a rung rather than after, since the point of knowing is to decide whether loop D can replay A, B and C at that support at all -- and SPAT-8 requires exactly that replay [step: rebuild_binaries]. A CONSERVATION IDENTITY THAT CAN FAIL, AND A DEFAULT WORTH INVERTING, 2026-08-22. ESMF states and tests itself against `sum(F_src*A_src*frac_src) == sum(F_dst*A_dst)` under destination-area normalisation, holding itself to about 1e-9 relative on analytic fields, with interpolation ACCURACY a separate and much looser 1e-2 test. THAT IS A CHECK WITH A RIGHT ANSWER and `lib/gridding.py` has never had one. Normalisation maps exactly onto this prefix's intensive/extensive split: destination-area for extensive totals, fraction-area for densities, and THE FRACTION FIELD MUST TRAVEL WITH THE RESULT because a fraction discarded is an identity that can no longer be evaluated -- the choice belongs to the FIELD, not to the call. THE DEFAULT WORTH INVERTING: ESMF errors on unmapped destination cells unless explicitly told to ignore them, checks the WEIGHT MATRIX rather than the intermediate search, and names the offending element id; the total-miss case has its own message so it cannot silently match zero cells. `lib/gridding.py:243-254` silently backfills empty cells from the nearest region centre and returns a count, with no threshold at which it refuses. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 55 |

The strandline band between the solved lake surface and the spill level is the ground a single climatology's lake COULD vacate; the design asks for the band it is measured to vacate over the 57-year component, which is the forcing slow enough for lake level to equilibrate | `pedology/notes/derived-surface-classes.md` | open, and blocked on CYC-1 rather than on effort. It cannot be faked from one climatology: a basin permanently full never exposes its bed and a basin permanently dry never accumulates one, so the whole class is about alternation. Re-run `build_surface_classes.py` against the cycle climatologies when they exist [step: surface_classes, stellar_cycle_run] |

| SURF-7 | Place groundwater silcrete and calcrete's groundwater-calcite pathway once a water table exists. `pedology/config/surface_classes.yaml` currently refuses groundwater silcrete by name, with the reason in the config: it sits at or near a water table and this project models none | `hydrography/notes/groundwater-scoping.md` section 6, `pedology/notes/derived-surface-classes.md` | open and UNBLOCKED: GW-1 closed 2026-08-20 and the `groundwater` step generates the field, so this is now work rather than a wait. GW-3 HAS since supplied the external test and the answer is regime-dependent rather than a blanket uncertification: no per-cell skill in the arid Australian case, Pearson +0.2599 on United States unconfined bores, and `sink_fraction` in `water_table.nc` separates the two per cell. The gate's measured AUC of 0.52 to 0.57 for this mask was taken over ALL cells, so re-measuring it restricted to cells where the sink did not set the depth is the cheap thing to try before treating the mask as dead. What becomes placeable is the MEAN depth field and a discharge mask, not Fenske et al. (2025)'s model: that hardens a layer over the RANGE of water table fluctuation at 10^5 years or longer, and both are durations `docs/src/reference/no-time-axis.md` refuses. Gypcrete is untouched, being surface and air processes rather than this mechanism [step: surface_classes]. GRAVITY CANCELS OUT OF THE DISCHARGE, WHICH DECIDES THIS ROW'S EXPOSURE, 2026-08-22. CaMa-Flood's local-inertial momentum equation collapses at steady state to `q = (1/n) h^(5/3) S^(1/2)` -- Manning, with gravity GONE. For a given depth, slope and roughness, steady-state discharge here is identical to Earth's, so A DISCHARGE MASK NEEDS NO GRAVITY CORRECTION. Gravity only sets the gravity-wave celerity, hence the transient and the stable timestep, so a flood-duration or wave-timing product would need it. The kinematic wave alternative has no gravity term at all. Timestep goes as `g^-1/2`, costing about 14 percent more substeps here. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 52b |

| VOLC-9 | Bracket the thermostat over WEATHERING SCHEME CHOICE, which currently carries no structural uncertainty at all. `pedology/scripts/thermostat_efficiency.py` returns a thermostat strength from one parameterisation and `config/planet.yaml` records that the outgassing plausibility check behind the prescribed pCO2 came from `weathering_fluxes.py`; neither has a bracket, and three published alternatives now sit on disk with their constants. `vendor/cgenie/genie-rokgem/data/input` ships Amiotte-Suchet 2003, Gibbs 1999 and GEM_CO2 as class lists plus constants tables, selected by `par_weathopt`. Gibbs is a different formulation on different units. IF THAT COLUMN READING IS RIGHT THE DISAGREEMENT IS THE THERMOSTAT ITSELF, since silicate weathering is the long-term sink and carbonate weathering returns its CO2 on marine precipitation, so the silicate fraction is what sets thermostat strength | `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 17, `pedology/analysis/thermostat_efficiency.json` | open. CONFIRM THE COLUMN MEANING FIRST: it is inferred from position and the schemes' published form rather than from a header, and a bracket built on a misread column is worse than no bracket. EXPLORATION: the deliverable is what scheme choice costs the thermostat number, not an adoption -- rokgem's global-rate mode is coarser than this project's lithology map and is not a candidate. THE CLASS SYSTEMS DO NOT MAP and that is most of the work: rokgem uses carbonate, shale, sandstone, basalt, shield and acid volcanics, while this world's land is playa clastic at about a quarter, continental clastic, schist, granite, granodiorite, melange and evaporite. There is NO land carbonate class here, LITH-26 having placed carbonate on the shelf, and playa clastic and evaporite have no counterpart in any of the three. So each scheme has to be mapped onto the classes this world has, and the mapping is itself a declared assumption that belongs in the bracket rather than under it [step: weathering_fluxes, thermostat_efficiency]. PRECONDITION MET AND THE PREMISE CORRECTED, 2026-08-22, from the code that CONSUMES the table rather than from column position. The reading of columns 3 and 4 was RIGHT -- `rokgem_box.f90:1842` names them "fCa and fSi" outright and they partition `dum_calcium_flux` at 1848 and 1858. Column 1 is the base rate, column 2 the runoff exponent, 5 to 7 osmium. BUT THE DISAGREEMENT THIS ROW EXPECTED IS NOT THERE: the two files the code actually reads carry IDENTICAL fCa/fSi, 0.93/0.07 carbonate, 0.39/0.61 shale, 0.48/0.52 sandstone, 0.00/1.00 basalt. The 1.00/0.00 values quoted here are in the four-column `Amiotte_2003_consts.dat` and `Gibbs_1999_consts.dat`, which the seven-column reader cannot consume. THERE ARE TWO SELECTABLE 2D SCHEMES, NOT THREE, and `rokgem.f90:69,76` names them: `GKWM` IS Gibbs 1999 and `GEM_CO2` IS Amiotte-Suchet 2003, so the four-column files are archival vintages of the same two. Base rates are not comparable across schemes -- 32000 against 1.586 -- because the conversion factors carry different units. THE BRACKET THIS ROW WANTS IS THE RUNOFF FUNCTIONAL FORM, which is structural rather than a constant: GKWM is `runoff ** k` with per-lithology exponents 0.68 to 0.91, GEM_CO2 is strictly LINEAR in runoff. Both normalise through `r_avg_runoff`, so they agree at mean runoff by construction and diverge in the TAILS. The fSi-weighted effective exponent is 0.720, giving 1.57x at a fifth of mean runoff and 0.64x at five times it, and a thermostat about 30 PERCENT WEAKER under GKWM, flat at 29 to 32 percent across runoff responses of 1.05 to 1.50 -- so it is usable as one bracket number rather than a curve. That is the deliverable this row asked for, reached through the exponent instead of through fCa/fSi. The class-mapping work this row describes still stands and is still most of the effort. TRAP, currently inert: `sub_load_weath` reads seven values list-directed and would read a four-column file ACROSS line boundaries and desynchronise silently rather than failing; nothing can select those files today, but this row currently treats them as the scheme definitions. `notes/external-model-survey.md` section 31 |
